-># THE DAWN<- <note>original: "AURORA"</note>

->## AND THE<-
->## TREASURE OF THE PHILOSOPHERS,<-
By Theophrastus Paracelsus, a German Philosopher and a Physician more accurate than all others.

There is added:
The Physical Monarchy <term>Monarchia Physica: A philosophical concept suggesting that nature is governed by a single, unified set of laws, much like a kingdom.</term> by **GERARD DORN**, in defense of the Paracelsian Principles established by his Teacher. <note>Gerard Dorn (c. 1530–1584) was an influential Belgian physician and alchemist who dedicated much of his life to explaining and defending the often-obscure writings of Paracelsus.</note>

Furthermore:
The **Living Anatomy** of Paracelsus, in which the author teaches that beyond the mere dissection of bodies, and before death, the suffering must be aided. <note>Paracelsus used the term "living anatomy" to argue that doctors should study the spiritual and chemical forces active in a living person, rather than relying solely on the study of corpses.</note>

<note>On the left, a circular library stamp, partially illegible, contains the words "Royal Library" (original: "BIBL REGIA").</note>

<note>In the center, a woodcut printer's device depicts a palm tree within an ornate frame. A banner entwined with the tree reads "The Palm of Guarinus" (original: "PALMA GVAR"). The palm tree was the trademark of the Basel-based printer Thomas Guarinus.</note>

1577.

AT BASEL.
T. <unclear>Guarinus</unclear>

<note>A decorative initial letter N depicts a figure set within a landscape.</note> It must be considered, most illustrious Prince, that it was not without Fate <note>original: "Non sine Fato"</note> that you bear the name of the most serene King Francis the Great of Valois, the first of that name, of pious and happy memory, your Highness’s grandfather. For in truth, you easily reflect all the gifts and virtues of his great soul: by summoning from everywhere men of powerful intellect, who dedicate their efforts to reviving, advancing, defending from insults, and bringing to the standard of Truth the liberal arts <note>term: "bonis artibus" refers to the "good arts" or academic disciplines intended to cultivate a free person's mind</note> in every branch of knowledge. Not undeservedly, therefore, if these men by their own...

4 Dedicatory Epistle.

modest night-studies <note>original: "lucubratiunculis" implies works produced through diligent study by candlelight</note> (God willing, however) have achieved anything, they wish it to be dedicated first to God their Benefactor, and then to such Princes as patrons <note>term: "Maecenatibus" refers to Gaius Maecenas, the Roman patron of the arts; the term became a standard word for a wealthy supporter of scholars</note>. That very thing both the late Henry II, most powerful King and father of pious memory, and now also Henry III, the most invincible King and brother—all Kings of happy France—have always held in high regard. For what can be more worthy of Kings and Princes than to season the arduous counsels of government with letters <note>meaning: literature and learning</note> as if with salt? This very thing bears witness, in this matter, to the exceptional brilliance of your mind: your great desire that a physical translation <note>term: "Translationem Physicam" here refers to a translation concerning "Physics" in the Aristotelian sense—the study of nature and medicine</note> be made of the German books of Theophrastus Paracelsus—the German philosopher who is the most acute and learned of men—so that through you, France might become more fruitful by this literary happiness. Since your Highness has deigned to appoint me (though unworthy) to this gift and duty: under your safe patronage, [I have] only [the most] fortu- <note>The text ends mid-word "fœli-" (fortunate/happy), a catchword meant to lead into the next page.</note>

### Dedicatory Epistle. 5

...tunate beginning <note>original: "fœliciter auspicari," completing the sentence from the previous page's catchword "fœli-"</note>; and I can do so especially because this labor directs its entire strength toward one goal: that the medical and physical arts—which have leaned upon the false foundations of the Heathens <note>term: "Aethnicorum" refers to the ancient Greek and Roman authorities, such as Galen and Aristotle, whose pagan theories dominated medieval medicine but were rejected by Paracelsians</note>—may return to their true origins. Truly, it seems to be of no small importance to look ahead and see which medicines and which physicians men—especially Kings and Princes—entrust their lives to. For the very drugs that have been in common use until now are unsound and prone to decay <note>original: "corruptioni," referring to the natural process of rotting or losing medicinal potency</note>. Indeed, even if they are set aside for a short time and not even swallowed, they grow "sickly" on their own; thus, they introduce diseases rather than driving them away as they should. For it is necessary that anything generated must be like its own source: just as disease comes from disease, so too health comes from health, and corruption from corruption. Therefore, our teacher Paracelsus teaches, by a far more excellent method, how to restore the parts of life that have been loosened and thrown into discord by corruption, using medicines that have been purged of all corruptibility through the spagyric art <note>term: "spagirica" (Spagyric) is a term coined by Paracelsus for alchemical medicine, derived from the Greek words for "to separate" and "to combine." It involves extracting the pure essence of a plant or mineral while discarding the toxic or useless "dross."</note>.

A 3

<summary>The author argues that traditional medicine based on ancient pagan foundations is corrupt and dangerous, and asserts that Paracelsus’s alchemical "spagyric" method offers a superior, incorruptible way to heal.</summary>

### 8 Dedicatory Epistle

...to lead back <note>original: "adducere," completing the thought from the previous page regarding the restoration of health</note> in manifold ways to the proportion of Unity and health, through a certain sympathy <note>term: "sympathy" (simpathia) refers to the Renaissance concept of "hidden strings" or resonance between the heavens, the earth, and the human body</note>: not from the schools of the unfaithful Heathens <note>original: "Aethnicorum," referring to pagan authorities like Galen or Aristotle whose theories dominated the universities</note>, but from the true and only source of all sciences and the center of Truth, of which the Heathens and their followers were utterly ignorant.

As will be made more clearly manifest in my *Monarchy* <note>term: "*Monarchia*" likely refers to the author's own treatise, *Monarchia Physico-medica*, intended to establish a sovereign system of medicine</note>: in which the true foundations of the medical art will be revealed by the most solid arguments, and those medical principles which Paracelsus brought to light shall be established upon an unshaken foundation. 

To conclude briefly: there is no art or all-encompassing wisdom that does not flow from the center of Unity and Truth, just as a line flows from a point. Receive therefore, I pray, Illustrious Prince, from the hands of your most humble servant, these things which until now have never been heard with such clarity; and protect them with your patronage against those "giant" men <note>original: "giganteos homines," a metaphor for the powerful academic authorities of the time who were seen as "giants" obstructing the path of new truth</note> who are enemies of the truth; defend them as best you can from injury and envy. Meanwhile, may Your Highness fare well in Jesus Christ, and live for all eternity.

<summary>The author concludes his dedication by arguing that true medical knowledge must flow from divine Unity rather than pagan traditions, and he asks the Duke for protection against academic critics.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, Medical Reform, Unity of Truth, Patronage, Heathen philosophy, Sympathy, Monarchy of Medicine</keywords>

<meta>This page begins the primary text of the "Aurora Philosophorum." Having concluded the dedicatory epistle by Gerard Dorn on the previous page, we now enter the treatise itself, which Paracelsus opens by rooting alchemical and natural knowledge in the biblical history of the first men.</meta>

THE DAWN
->The Dawn of the Philosophers by Doctor<-
->THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS.<- <note>original: "Aurora Philosophorum." The title "Aurora" (Dawn) symbolizes the awakening of new wisdom and the revealing of hidden secrets in the light of nature.</note>

# Chapter One.

## On the Origin of the Philosophic Stone. <note>original: "Lapis Philosophici." The "Stone" is the ultimate goal of alchemy, a substance capable of perfecting both metals and the human body.</note>

<note>A decorative initial letter 'A' begins the paragraph, typically featuring ornate woodcut floral designs or mythical figures common in 16th-century printing.</note> Adam, the first inventor of the arts, because he possessed the knowledge of all things—both after the Fall and before it—foresaw that the world would eventually pass through water <note>A reference to the Great Flood</note>. For this very reason, it came to pass that his successors erected two stone tables <note>or tablets</note> upon which they carved all the natural arts in **hieroglyphic characters**, so that this prophecy might be known to posterity and observed, and that provision might be made in times of danger.

Afterward, Noah found one of these tables in Armenia beneath Mount Ararat <note>original: "Araroth"</note> once the Flood had passed. On this table were marked the courses of the upper firmament <note>the heavens</note> and the lower sphere <note>the earth</note>, as well as the paths of the planets. Finally, this universal knowledge, being split apart into various pieces, was diminished in its power; for such a separation made one man an astronomer, another a mage, another a cabalist <note>A practitioner of Cabala, a mystical and interpretive tradition often linked by Paracelsus to the secrets of nature</note>, and a fourth an alchemist.

That "Vulcan-like" <note>original: "Vulcanicus." Vulcan was the Roman god of fire and smithing; in alchemy, fire is the primary tool of transformation</note> Abraham, [who was also] Tubal-Cain <note>Paracelsus here links the biblical patriarch Abraham with Tubal-Cain, described in Genesis as the first smith, suggesting a hidden lineage of metallurgical wisdom</note>, the greatest astrologer and arithmetician, brought [this knowledge] out of Egypt into the land of Canaan,

<summary>This page introduces the legendary origin of alchemical knowledge, claiming it was first held by Adam and preserved through the Great Flood on stone tablets discovered by Noah.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Noah, Mount Ararat, alchemy, astronomy, magic, Cabala, Abraham, Tubal-Cain, philosophic stone, hieroglyphics</keywords>

OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 9

...produced <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "the separation of knowledge produced a cabalist."</note>, from which the Egyptians rose to such a height of excellence that the wisdom of this same matter passed from them even to other nations. Since indeed, even the Patriarch Jacob’s act of coloring sheep with various patterns <note>original: "oues picturasse varijs coloribus". A reference to Genesis 30:37–39, where Jacob used peeled rods to influence the markings of his flock, which Paracelsus interprets here as a feat of natural magic.</note> was performed through a branch of magic. For the Chaldeans, Hebrews, Persians, and Egyptians always set forth these liberal arts—alongside Theology—to their nobles and priests as the highest philosophy to be mastered.

It happened no differently in the times of Moses, during which both the priests and the physicians themselves were chosen from among the **magi** <note>original: "magos". In this context, Paracelsus refers to "magi" not as sorcerers, but as wise men and scholars of the hidden secrets of nature.</note>: the former indeed were chosen for their judgment concerning matters of health, especially in the diagnosis of leprosy. Moses himself was also instructed in the schools of the Egyptians through the care and expense of Pharaoh’s daughter, so that he might excel in their wisdom. In the same way, Daniel from his earliest youth drank deeply of the doctrine of the Chaldeans, so that he might emerge as a **cabalist** <note>original: "cabalistam". A practitioner of the Cabala, a mystical system of interpreting scripture and nature through hidden meanings and numbers.</note>.

His divine prophecies and his interpretations of those words, **"Numbered, Numbered, Weighed, Divided,"** bear witness to this. <note>original: "Mene Mene Tecelphares". These are the Aramaic words found in Daniel 5:25, appearing as supernatural writing on the wall during Belshazzar's feast.</note> These words are to be understood through the prophetic and cabalistic art. The tradition of this cabalistic art was very familiar and frequently practiced among Moses and the prophets. The prophet Elijah predicted many things through his cabalistic numbers. Likewise, the ancient sages learned to know God rightly through this natural and mystical art; by laws of this kind, they stood more firmly in His commandments and walked...

A 5 <note>A signature mark used by early printers to help binders assemble the pages of the book in the correct order.</note>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that the hidden arts of magic and cabala were foundational to the wisdom of biblical figures like Jacob, Moses, and Daniel. He presents these "liberal arts" as a legitimate and essential part of the education of ancient priests and physicians.</summary>
<keywords>Egyptians, Jacob, Moses, Daniel, Cabala, magic, natural philosophy, leprosy, Mene Mene Tekel Upharsin, Elijah, ancient wisdom</keywords>

10 &emsp;&emsp;&emsp;&emsp;&emsp;&emsp; **THE DAWN** <note>original: "AVRORA". This is the title of the work, *Aurora Philosophorum* (The Dawn of the Philosophers).</note>

[decl]ared. <note>This fragment completes a word from the previous page.</note> Similarly, it is held in the Book of Samuel that those of the Beelistic order <note>original: "Beelisticos". Likely referring to diviners or those traditionally associated with the spirit of Baal or Beelzebub; Paracelsus argues here that their abilities were not necessarily demonic.</note> did not follow the fate of the Devil, but by divine permission were participants in true visions and apparitions. We shall indeed press this point more extensively in our book *On Supercelestial Matters*. A gift of this kind was granted by the Lord God to priests walking in divine precepts. It was the custom among the Persians to admit no one as king unless he was exalted as a wise man <note>original: "sophum". From the Greek *sophos*.</note> in both deed and name; this is certainly evident by this very name of their kings, **Sophi** <note>original: "Sophi". A reference to the Persian Safavid rulers, whom Paracelsus identifies with the Greek concept of the "wise man."</note>. Such also were those Persian wise men and **magi** who sought Christ Jesus from the East, and they were called **Natural Priests** <note>original: "sacerdotes naturales". These are scholars who study the "Light of Nature"—the divine wisdom hidden within the physical world—rather than just religious dogma.</note>. The Egyptians also, having attained this magic and philosophy from the Chaldeans and Persians, desired their own priests to learn the same wisdom. In this pursuit they produced so much fruit that to all the neighboring regions [they became...] <note>The text breaks off at the fragment "ven", likely the start of "venerabiles" (venerable) or "venditarent" (offered/made known).</note>

<summary>This page explores the divine origin of natural magic, arguing that Persian "Sophi" and the biblical Magi were "Natural Priests" who used God-given wisdom to understand the world.</summary>

OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 11

[P]ostmodum <note>original: "Postmodum". Afterwards.</note> when the son of Noah <note>Historically, alchemical traditions often identified this son as Ham (or Cham), who was believed to have preserved the antediluvian sciences and brought them to Egypt.</note> occupied the third part of the world after the Flood, **this art** <note>The "Hermetic Art" or alchemy, which the author treats as a singular, divine science of nature.</note> burst with a certain force into Chaldea and Persia, and from there into Egypt. When the superstitious and idolatrous Greeks caught wind of this, those among them who were more clever betook themselves to the Chaldeans and Egyptians, so that they might drink the same wisdom from their schools.

But in truth, since the theological or biblical study of the Mosaic law <note>The Law of Moses.</note> did not please them, they relied on their own genius and fell away from the right foundation of such natural secrets and arts. This is clear by example, as they viewed the doctrine of Moses through the lens of myths <note>original: "fabulosè". The author argues that the Greeks turned historical and spiritual truths into mere fables or "fabulae."</note> and faltered. 

It was the custom of the Egyptians to present such traditions of excellent wisdom only through figures, enigmas, and abstruse stories and terms. These things **Homer** afterwards shadowed forth with the wondrous artifice of his poems. Then came **Pythagoras**, who mixed in many things from the Law of Moses and the Old Testament. Likewise, **Hippocrates**, **Thales of Miletus**, **Anaxagoras**, **Democritus**, and others did not cease to apply their own wits to these matters. Although none of them were truly skilled in the true <term>Astrology</term> <note>In the Paracelsian tradition, true Astrology is the understanding of the "internal stars" or the spiritual forces that govern both the cosmos and the human body.</note>, Geometry, Arithmetic, or Medicine. Their pride surely hindered them, as it would not allow them to accept being students of other nations. And when they had perceived something from the Chaldeans and Egyptians, they became far more proud than... <note>The sentence continues on the following page.</note>

<summary>This page traces the migration of alchemical and philosophical wisdom from the descendants of Noah to the Egyptians and eventually to the Greeks. The author criticizes the Greek philosophers for corrupting this divine knowledge with pride and mythology, failing to ground it in the biblical tradition of Moses.</summary>

### OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 11

Afterwards <note>original: "Postmodum"</note>, when the son of Noah <note>In alchemical tradition, this is usually identified as Ham, who was believed to have preserved the antediluvian sciences and carried them to Egypt.</note> occupied the third part of the world after the Flood, **this art** <note>The "Hermetic Art" or alchemy, which the author treats as a singular, divine science of nature.</note> burst with a certain force into Chaldea and Persia, and from there into Egypt. When the superstitious and idolatrous Greeks caught wind of this, those among them who were more clever betook themselves to the Chaldeans and Egyptians, so that they might drink the same wisdom from their schools.

But in truth, since the theological or biblical study of the Mosaic law <note>The Law of Moses.</note> did not please them, they relied on their own genius and fell away from the right foundation of such natural secrets and arts. This is clear by example, as they viewed the doctrine of Moses through the lens of myths <note>original: "fabulosè". The author argues that the Greeks turned historical and spiritual truths into mere fables or "fabulae."</note> and faltered. 

It was the custom of the Egyptians to present such traditions of excellent wisdom only through figures, enigmas, and abstruse stories and terms. These things **Homer** afterwards shadowed forth with the wondrous artifice of his poems. Then came **Pythagoras**, who mixed in many things from the Law of Moses and the Old Testament. <meta>This page continues the discussion of the Greek philosophers' relationship with ancient Egyptian and Eastern wisdom. It concludes the critique of the Greeks and begins Chapter 4, which examines the nature of the true Magi among the Chaldeans, Persians, and Egyptians.</meta>

->### OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 13<-

concerning these matters, they stood out as discordant. **Pythagoras**, for that reason, did not wish to be called a **Sophus** <note>original: "Sophus"; meaning a Sage or Wise Man. Pythagoras famously coined the term "philosopher" (lover of wisdom) to humbly suggest he was a seeker rather than a possessor of full wisdom.</note>, because the Egyptian Priesthood and their wisdom were by no means taught perfectly, as was fitting; and even if he had nevertheless brought back many mysteries and secrets from there, **Anaxagoras** likewise brought back very many. This is clear from the disputations which he conducted concerning the **sun** and its **stone** <note>original: "de sole, & eius lapide". This likely refers to alchemical theories regarding the "Solar Stone" or the "Philosopher's Stone" as it relates to the celestial properties of the sun.</note>, and which he left behind at his death. 

Nevertheless, he was contrary to the Egyptians in many things. For which reason they also did not wish to be called **Sages**, nor **Magi**, but imitating Pythagoras in that matter, they assumed the name of **philosophy**; yet they themselves brought back nothing but certain sparks, like shadows, from the **Magic of the Persians and Egyptians**.

But in truth, **Moses**, **Abraham**, **Solomon**, **Adam**, **Elijah**, and the **Magi** coming from the East to Christ, were the true Magi and divine Sages, as well as **Kabbalists** <note>The author links biblical patriarchs to Kabbalah, a Jewish mystical tradition involving the secret interpretation of scripture and the nature of the divine.</note>. In this art and wisdom, the Greeks had little or no experience at all. Therefore, we leave behind the philosophical wisdom of those Greeks as mere speculation, being far and wide distant and separated from the other true arts and sciences.

### Chapter Four.
### What sort of Magi the Chaldeans, Persians, and Egyptians were.

**M**any have attempted both to scrutinize and to put into practice the most secret magic of these wise men. However, this very thing has so far been done...

<summary>This page argues that Greek "philosophy" is merely a shadow of the true divine wisdom held by biblical patriarchs and Eastern Magi. It marks the transition from critiquing Greek speculation to exploring the authentic magical traditions of the Chaldeans, Persians, and Egyptians.</summary>
<keywords>Pythagoras, Anaxagoras, Sophus, Sage, Magi, Alchemical Sun, Stone of the Sun, Kabbalah, Persian Magic, Egyptian Wisdom, Greek Philosophy vs. Divine Science</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 40 regarding the true nature of wisdom, contrasting the limited knowledge of the Greeks with the "divine sages" and the works of contemporary occultists.</meta>

41 ->AURORA<-

did not come to pass. Many in our own time exalt **Trithemius**, others **Bacon**, and **Agrippa**, for their **Magic** and **Kabbalah** <note>The author refers to Johannes Trithemius (an abbot and occultist), Roger Bacon (the medieval philosopher), and Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (author of *Three Books of Occult Philosophy*), who were central figures in Renaissance esoteric thought.</note>, which seem to agree very little with one another, because they are ignorant of why they do so. **Magic** is indeed the art and faculty through which one reaches the hidden properties, powers, and operations of the **elements**, **bodies**, and their **fruits**.

But **Kabbalah** itself, through **anagogy** <note>original: "anagogia"; a method of mystical interpretation that seeks to find a higher, spiritual, or "uplifting" meaning in a text or reality.</note>, seems to build a path to God for men: how one must deal with Him, and how to prophesy from Him. For Kabbalah is full of **divine mysteries**, just as Magic is full of **natural secrets**; indeed, it teaches how to utter predictions from the nature of things present and future. 

For its operation consists in knowing all creatures—both **celestial** and **terrestrial** bodies—from within: what is hidden in them, what powers lie concealed, to what they were destined from the beginning, and with what properties they were endowed. These and similar things are the **bond** by which celestial things are joined to terrestrial things, as can even sometimes be perceived visually and through the senses by their operations. 

Such a conjunction of celestial influences, or the impression by which celestial powers operate upon the lower bodies, was once called **Gamahea** <note>original: "Gamahea"; also known as *Gamaheu* or *Camaieu*, these are naturally occurring stones or talismans believed to bear the "signature" or image of celestial forces and possess occult healing or magical powers.</note> by the Magi; it was also called the **marriage** of celestial powers and properties with elemental bodies. From this flowed the most excellent mixtures of all celestial bodies, and terre- <note>The text cuts off mid-word, likely continuing as "terrestrial" on the following page.</note>

->Chapter five.<-
->Concerning the most powerful and supreme essence of things.<-

The **Magi** <note>original: "Magi"; ancient wise men or practitioners of "high magic," which was then considered a form of deep natural science.</note>, through their wisdom, affirmed that all creatures are to be brought back to a **Unified substance** <note>original: "Unitam substantiam".</note>. They assert that through its own cleansings and purifications, this substance ascends to such a degree of subtlety, divine nature, and hidden property that it performs wonders. For they considered that it would return to the earth, and through the supreme magical separation, a certain perfect substance would emerge. This substance is at last—by many industrious and very lengthy preparations—raised from vegetable substances into minerals, from minerals into metallic forms, and from perfect metallic substances into a perpetual and divine **Fifth Essence** <note>original: "essentiam quintam"; the Quintessence, the purest and most potent state of matter, believed to be the "spirit" of the world and a universal medicine.</note>, which contains within itself the essence of all celestial and earthly creatures...

OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 17

concluding <note>This word "concludentem" completes a sentence from the previous page regarding the unified substance.</note>. The Arabs and Greeks, through hidden characters and the **hieroglyphic** <note>original: "Hieroglyphicas"; in the 16th century, hieroglyphs were viewed as mystical symbols encoding divine secrets rather than a phonetic writing system.</note> descriptions of the Persians and Egyptians, arrived at secret and hidden mysteries. Having attained these—partly understood and partly not—they saw certain wonders with their own eyes through experimentation. Whoever attained this stone shrouded it in various enigmatic figures, deceptive similarities, comparisons, and fictional names, so that its material might at least remain hidden from posterity, and not even the slightest knowledge of it could be easily obtained.

->Chapter six.<-
->Concerning the various errors regarding the material, its discovery, and knowledge of it.<-

**Philosophers** have assigned various and most hidden names to this material of the stone, drawn from similarities. **Arnaldus** <note>Arnaldus de Villa Nova (c. 1240–1311), a celebrated physician and alchemist whose works were central to the medieval tradition.</note>, observing this in his *Rosary* <note>original: "Rosario"; referring to the *Rosarium Philosophorum*, a famous alchemical treatise.</note>, says that the greatest difficulty exists in the discovery of the material of this stone. For they called it **vegetable**, **mineral**, and **animal**, yet not according to the literal sense, as those wise in divine secrets know.

...and have experienced the miracles of such a stone. An example of this is offered by **Raymund Lully’s** <note>Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1315), a Majorcan philosopher and polymath to whom many later alchemical works were attributed.</note> **lunaria** <note>original: "lunaria"; literally "moon-plant," here referring to a specific alchemical substance or process named after the plant for its perceived lunar (silver-making) qualities.</note>, which produces flowers of wonderful virtues familiar to the philosophers themselves. No small error has also arisen regarding the **juices of vegetables** <note>original: "vegetabilium liquoribus"; botanical extracts or saps.</note>, with which many have attempted to **coagulate mercury** <note>To turn liquid quicksilver into a solid form, a key step in many alchemical recipes.</note>, and afterwards convert it into **silver** <note>original: "luna" (the moon); the standard alchemical and astrological term for silver.</note> with **fixing waters** <note>Chemical solutions intended to "fix" a substance, making it resistant to heat or evaporation.</note>. They believed that anyone who could coagulate mercury in this way, without the help of other metals, had achieved the greatest **mastery** <note>original: "magisterium"; the successful completion of the alchemical Great Work.</note>.

And although the juices of some vegetables do achieve this effect, it should be judged to happen for no other reason than because of the **resin**, **fatness**, or **earthy sulfur** in which they abound. This draws the humidity and life of the mercury to itself, mixing with that substance and coagulating it, yet without any actual utility. For I have found that no thick and external sulfur found in vegetables is fit for a perfect projection in alchemy; this is something many have experienced, and not without the greatest expense.

### Chapter seven.
### Concerning the errors of those who seek the stone from vegetables, as mentioned above.

Some alchemists boiled the juice expressed from celandine to a thickness or consistency, and exposed it to the sun so that it might coagulate into a hard mass by itself. This was afterwards ground into a very fine powder of a black or reddish-brown color, which they hoped would turn mercury into **gold** <note>original: "solem" (the sun); the alchemical and astrological term for gold.</note> through **projection** <note>The final stage of the alchemical process where the Philosopher's Stone is "projected" or cast onto base metals to transmute them.</note>; but they discovered this to be useless. Others mixed **sal ammoniac** <note>original: "sal armoniacum"; ammonium chloride, often used in alchemy as a flux or to help substances "penetrate" metals.</note> with this powder; others used **colcothar of vitriol** <note>original: "colchotar vitrioli"; the brownish-red iron oxide residue left after the distillation of sulfuric acid (oil of vitriol).</note>, believing that in this way they could reach their desired goal. They brought these solutions into a yellow water, so that the sal ammoniac might grant the tincture entry into the substance of the mercury...

### Chapter Eight.

*Concerning those who sought the Philosopher’s Stone in animal substances.*

**T**hrough a certain metaphorical naming, they said their material was **Virgin’s milk** <note>original: "lac virginis"; a common alchemical term for a white liquid or solvent, often referring to a purified mercury or a specific acid.</note> and the **blessed blood of rosy color** <note>original: "rosei coloris benedictum sanguinem".</note>, which however is suited only to the prophets and the sons of God. From this, the **Sophists** <note>In alchemical texts, "Sophists" refers to false practitioners or "puffers" who took metaphors literally and lacked true philosophical understanding.</note> concluded that this philosophical matter exists in the blood of animals or of man, taking the opportunity [of the idea] that they are nourished from vegetables. Others [sought it] in hair, in the salt of urine, and in **Rebis** <note>original: "Rebis"; literally "the double thing," usually referring to the hermaphroditic end-product of the alchemical process, but here likely referring to bodily excretions or waste products containing "two things."</note>. Some extracted salt from stinking **wash** <note>original: "lotio"; referring to urine, which was a common source of ammonia and salts for early chemists.</note>, believing it to be the matter of the Stone. There were even those who judged the small stones found in **Rebis** <note>Likely referring to kidney or bladder stones, which were occasionally examined for alchemical properties.</note> to be the matter. Others soaked egg membranes in the harshest **lye** <note>original: "lixiuio"; a strong alkaline solution made from wood ashes.</note>, with which they also mixed the calcined shells of eggs, white as snow, attributing to these the **secret** <note>original: "arcanum".</note> of fixation for the purpose of transforming mercury. Others, comparing the white of eggs to silver and the yolk to gold, chose them for their material, mixing in common salt, **sal ammoniac** <note>original: "armoniaco"; ammonium chloride, a volatile salt used as a flux in metallurgical and alchemical operations.</note>, and **tartar** <note>original: "tartaro"; a potassium salt that forms as a crust in wine barrels, frequently used as a chemical reagent.</note>...

This one they fed with the filings of copper or electrum, believing that this kind of animal, through the power of its digestion alone, could effect the desired transformation in its stomach. Finally, they would burn this animal into a red powder, which they thought was gold, but in this too they were deceived. Others finally sought it in fish, which are commonly called **Trout**... OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 23

...to call [them], when they were burned through **liquefaction** <note>original: "liquationem"; a process of melting or reducing a substance to a liquid state to separate components.</note>, they sometimes found gold, for no other reason than that this kind of fish, in rivers and torrents, was accustomed to collect and eat scales and golden sparks; however, this rarely happened. Such impostors are primarily found in the Courts of Princes. The matter of the philosophers is not to be sought in animals; I would wish everyone to be warned of this.

Yet it should be known that the philosophers called their Stone "animal" <note>In alchemy, the term "animal" often relates to the "anima" or soul. The author is explaining that the Stone is called animal not because it is made of meat or blood, but because it behaves like a living thing with a soul.</note> because in its final operations, by the power of this most noble fiery mystery, a dark liquor sweats out from that matter drop by drop in its vessel. From this, they brought forth this prophecy: namely, that in the final times, a most pure man would come into the lands, through whom the liberation of the world would occur; this very man would shed drops of a rosy or red color and of blood, by which means he would redeem the world from its stain <note>The author is drawing a direct parallel between the alchemical "blood" of the Stone and the blood of Christ, suggesting that the transformation of metals mirrors the spiritual redemption of humanity.</note>.

In like manner, the blood of their Stone (yet after its own kind) liberated **leprous metals** <note>original: "leprosa metalla"; a common alchemical term for base metals like lead or tin, which were viewed as "diseased" or "imperfect" versions of gold.</note> from their infirmities and contagion. Therefore, they thought they had not spoken without merit in saying their Stone was "animal." Concerning this mystery, **Hermes** <note>original: "Mercurius"; refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of the Hermetic tradition and alchemy.</note> speaks thus to **King Khalid** <note>original: "Regem Calid"; Khalid ibn Yazid, an early historical figure in Arabic alchemy often cited in Western medieval texts.</note>: "This mystery is permitted only to the Prophets of God to know, from which it follows that this Stone is called 'animal.'" For in the blood of this Stone, its soul lies hidden; it is also composed of a body, a spirit...

### Chapter nine.
### Concerning those who have sought the stone in animals.

The ignorant have come forward, believing the stone to be threefold, and hidden in a threefold genus: namely vegetable, animal, and mineral. Thus it happened that they sought it in minerals <note>The OCR reads "mineralibus" (minerals), though the chapter title specifies "animalibus" (animals); the author is likely addressing the error of searching for the Stone in any of these literal physical categories.</note>. This opinion is far removed from the view of the philosophers. For they [the true philosophers] assert that their stone is uniformly vegetable, animal, and mineral <note>Alchemists believed the Stone was "vegetable" because it grows, "animal" because it has a soul/life-force, and "mineral" because of its physical state.</note>. 

It should be noted in addition to this, that nature has distributed its mineral **sperm** <term>sperma: In alchemy, 'sperm' refers to the fundamental seed, vapor, or "life-force" that allows minerals to grow in the earth, rather than a biological fluid.</term> into various kinds: namely into sulfurs, salts, boraxes, nitres, ammoniacs, alums, arsenics, atraments <note>original: "atramenta"; a term for black vitriols often used for making inks.</note>, vitriols, tutias <note>original: "tutias"; zinc oxide, usually found as a crust in smelting furnaces.</note>, hematites, orpiments <note>original: "auripigmenta"; a gold-colored arsenic sulfide.</note>, realgars <note>original: "realgara"; a ruby-red arsenic sulfide.</note>, magnesias, cinnabar, antimony, talc, cachymia <term>cachymia: a term used by Paracelsus to describe an 'imperfect' metallic ore or the dross of metals that has not yet reached a pure state.</term>, marcasites, etc. In all of these, nature has not yet attained our matter. Although in some of the said species, it reveals itself in a wondrous appearance regarding the transmutation of imperfect metals that are to be brought to perfection. For long experience and fiery practice show a manifold change in the matter of minerals; not only from colors into

colors, but also from one essence into another, and from its own imperfection into perfection. Although nature, by means of mineral intermediaries, has pursued some perfection through preparations, the philosophers do not, for that reason, intend for the matter of their **Philosopher’s Stone** to proceed from any [literal] mineral, even though they say their Stone is mineral. 

From this, the **Sophists** <note>original: "Sophistae"; in this context, it refers to false alchemists or "puffers" who followed the literal letter of alchemical texts without understanding their spiritual or hidden meaning.</note>, taking this as an opportunity, have persecuted mercury itself with various tortures: some by sublimations, coagulations, precipitations, and by mercurial or strong waters <term>aqua fortis: literally "strong water," usually referring to nitric acid used to dissolve metals</term>, and so on. All these erroneous ways must be avoided, along with the other mineral preparations of the Sophists, such as their purifications and the fixations of spirits and metals. 

For this reason, all preparations of the Stone—such as those described by **Geber** <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–815), the celebrated Arab alchemist whose works were foundational to European alchemy.</note>, **Albertus Magnus**, and their like—are sophistic. Their purifications, cementations, sublimations, distillations, rectifications, circulations, putrefactions, conjunctions, solutions, ascensions, coagulations, calcinations, and **incerations** <term>inceration: the process of adding liquid to a dry substance to bring it to a waxy, soft state</term> are utterly useless. 

This applies whether these processes are performed in a tripod, an **athanor** <term>athanor: a furnace designed to maintain a uniform heat over a long period, also called a "palingenetic furnace"</term>, a reverberatory furnace, a melting furnace, an *accidioneo* <note>a specialized alchemical furnace or vessel</note>, or using [the heat of] dung, ash, sand, and the like; or whether they use a **cucurbit** <term>cucurbit: a gourd-shaped chemical vessel used in distillation</term>, a **pelican** <term>pelican: a circulatory vessel with two "arms" that feed the condensed liquid back into the body, symbolizing self-refining</term>, a retort, a vial, a fixatory vessel, and so on. 

The same judgment must be held concerning the sublimation of mercury through mineral spirits to reach the white and the red [stages of the Work], such as by use of vitriol, saltpeter, alum, or **saffron of Mars** <term>crocus Martis: a name for iron oxide or rust</term>. Concerning all these...

88 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**AURORA**

That Sophist, Johannes de Rupescissa <note>Jean de Roquetaillade (c. 1310–1366), a Franciscan friar and alchemist famous for his work on the "quintessence."</note>, tells false tales in his treatise on the philosophers' stone, both the white and the red. These are all nothing but fabricated dreams. One must also avoid the sophistic particulars <note>original: "particularia"; in alchemy, these are specific recipes intended to produce a limited result or a "partial" transmutation, rather than the "Universal" Great Work.</note> of Geber <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–815), the foundational figure of Arabic alchemy.</note>, such as the sevenfold sublimations, or the mortifications and likewise the revivifications of Mercury, along with their preparations made by means of salt of urine or the "sepulcher" <note>original: "sepulchrum"; a term often referring to a heat-source like a dung-pit or a specific vessel used for the "death" (putrefaction) of the matter.</note>, all of which are false. Certain others, having attempted to fix mercury with the sulfurs of minerals and metals, were utterly deceived. Indeed, I have seen mercury brought into a metallic body through such fixations which, in every likeness and in every respect, simulated and represented good silver; but in the test <term>testa: the cupel or vessel used in assaying; a trial by fire that reveals the purity of precious metals</term>, it appeared exactly as it was: false.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**Chapter Ten.**

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;*Concerning those who sought the stone and particulars*
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;*within minerals.*

**S**ome of the Sophists attempted to squeeze out a fixed oil from mercury that had been sublimated seven times and dissolved as many times, using "strong waters" <term>aqua fortis: nitric acid, used to dissolve metals</term> as a medium, so that imperfect metals might be brought to perfection: but they were forced to abandon this fruitless labor. Some purged vitriol <note>A group of sulfate minerals, often copper or iron sulfate, used as a primary reagent in alchemical laboratories.</note> seven times through calcination, solution, and coagulation, and with the addition of two parts of sal ammoniac <term>sal armoniacum: ammonium chloride, often used as a flux or to make "spirit of ammonia"</term> and sublimation, so that they might resolve it into white water...

->OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 27<-

...is prepared <note>original: "tur"; the suffix of a Latin passive verb concluding a sentence from the previous page</note>, to which they added a third part of quicksilver <note>original: "mercurij viui"; literally "living mercury," referring to liquid, metallic mercury.</note> so that the water might be coagulated. Afterward, they sublimated the mercury so many times with the aforementioned vitriol <term>vitriol: a sulfate of metal, usually iron or copper, often appearing as green or blue crystals</term> and sal ammoniac <term>sal ammoniac: ammonium chloride, a pungent salt used in alchemy to help "cleanse" or "spiritualize" metals through sublimation</term> that it was turned into a stone. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Others coagulated mercury using alum-water <term>alum: a mineral salt used for its astringent properties and to "fix" colors or substances</term> into a hard mass resembling alum itself, which they then attempted to fix <term>fixation: the process of making a volatile substance (like mercury) stable and resistant to the heat of fire</term> using "fixing waters," but it was useless. These Sophists set before themselves many reasons for fixing mercury, all in vain, because nothing of a truly perfected or stable nature is found within mercury when treated this way. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Thus it happens that minerals are added to it in vain through these sophistic processes. By such methods, the mercury is only provoked to a greater "malice" <note>original: "malitiam"; referring to the volatile, "unruly," and chemically aggressive nature of mercury that resists stabilization</note>; it is made more "alive" <note>meaning more liquid or volatile</note> and is brought toward a state of greater impurity rather than toward any perfection. Therefore, the true matter of the philosophers should not be sought from it. Because it is something imperfect, even if it could be brought to perfection, it would be extremely difficult—nay, impossible—by the methods of the Sophists, since there is nothing within it that can be stirred or forced into a state of perfection. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Some others took arsenic that had been sublimated several times, dissolved and coagulated it many times with oil of tartar <term>oil of tartar: a concentrated, caustic solution of potassium carbonate</term>, and attempted to "fix" it so they might turn copper into silver. This effort produced nothing except a certain "sophistic whitening" <note>A surface-level change where the metal looks silver but remains copper underneath; a common deception in pseudo-alchemy.</note>.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Wherefore I make known to all that the **whitening** <note>original: "dealbationem"; the process of making a "base" metal like copper look white like silver</note> of which mention was made is drawn from a false foundation; by it, copper is falsely whitened but not changed. The **Sophists** <note>In this context, "Sophists" refers to fraudulent alchemists or "puffers" who used chemistry to deceive rather than seek philosophical truth.</note> have mixed **Venus** <note>original: "venerem"; the alchemical name for copper</note>—masked and painted in this manner—with twice as much of their own **Moon** <note>original: "lunæ"; the alchemical name for silver</note>, and sold it to goldsmiths and minters. But in the end, they transformed themselves into counterfeiters—not only those who sold it, but even those who bought it. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Some of these sophists took **red arsenic** in place of the white, and this itself resulted in a false art. For in whatever way it is prepared, it exhibits nothing but whiteness. Others proceeded further to **common sulfur**, which is yellow; they cooked it in vinegar, lye, or very sharp urines day and night until it emerged white. Afterward, they raised it from common salt and **lime of eggshells** <note>original: "calce ouorum"; powdered, calcined eggshells used as a mild alkali or drying agent</note> through **sublimation** <term>sublimation: the process of heating a solid until it turns into a vapor and then solidifies again, used here to "purify" the sulfur</term>; they cooked and sublimated it many times. However, from that process, the best and most beautiful **cinnabar** <term>cinnabar: a bright red mineral (mercury sulfide) used as a pigment and source of mercury</term> resulted—the finest of all I have ever seen. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Just as there is an infinite number of minerals, so too is there a great variety in their preparations. Likewise, beware of the "oils" made from imperfect metals, as well as the perfect ones—gold and silver. For even if the operation of these substances is most powerful in the nature of things, nevertheless, the true process has remained known to very few, even to this day. One should also abstain from the sophistic preparations of common mercury, arsenic, sulfur, and other similar things—namely, those performed through **sublimation** <term>sublimation: heating a solid until it turns to vapor and then solidifies again to purify it</term>, through **descent** <term>descensum: a method of distillation where the vapor is forced downwards into a receiver</term>, and fixation with vinegar, **saltpeter** <note>original: "sale petræ"; potassium nitrate</note>, tartar, vitriol, and **sal ammoniac** <term>sal ammoniac: ammonium chloride, a salt frequently used as a flux or to "cleanse" metals</term>,

## 30 THE DAWN

[sal ammo]niac <note>original: "niaco"; likely completing "sal-ammoniaco" from the previous page</note>, yet by that way and reasoning which the books of the **sophists** <term>sophists: in this context, false alchemists or "puffers" who followed deceptive or superficial recipes</term> teach. Likewise, flee those sophistic **tinctures** <term>tincture: a substance or "medicine" capable of changing the color and quality of a metal</term> derived from **marcasites** <term>marcasite: a mineral (iron sulfide) often confused with gold or silver ore due to its metallic luster</term> and **saffron of Mars** <term>saffron of Mars (crocus Martis): a reddish-brown iron oxide (rust) used in alchemical preparations</term>, and from that fraud which goes by the name **part with part** <note>original: "partis cum parte"; an alchemical process of alloying or joining metals, often used to stretch gold or silver with base metals</note>, of "fixed moon," and similar trifles. For even if they exhibit some superficial appearance of truth (and indeed, this **fixation of the moon** <note>Moon (luna) is the alchemical name for silver. To "fix" it means to make it stable and resistant to fire.</note> requires little labor or industry), nevertheless, such a path of preparation is null and invalid.

Moved by compassion, therefore, toward the well-meaning workers of this art, I wished to reveal the true foundation of philosophy, explained through three **particular secrets** <term>particular secret: a specific alchemical recipe that works on certain metals, as opposed to the "Universal" Stone which transforms all things</term>: namely, union through **arsenic**; another through **vitriol**; and the third through **antimony**. From these, I shall teach the true **projection** <term>projection: the final step of alchemy where the "medicine" is cast onto a molten base metal to transform it into gold or silver</term> upon mercury and the imperfect metals.

### Chapter Eleven.
### Concerning the true and perfect particular secret from arsenic for the white tincture.

Some have written that **arsenic** is composed of mercury and sulfur; others, of earth and water; most, of the nature of sulfur. In whatever way it may be, its nature is such that it transmutes red copper into white. It can even be brought to such perfection of preparation that it truly and perfectly **colors** <note>original: "tingat"; to dye or permeate the metal to change its nature</note>. Not, however, by that way taught by the wicked sophists, such as **Geber** <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–815), a prolific scholar; the "Sum of Perfection" mentioned here is a famous Latin work attributed to him</note> in his *Sum of Perfection*, or **Albertus Magnus** <note>Albert the Great (c. 1200–1280), a renowned theologian and scientist whose name was often attached to alchemical manuscripts</note>,

->OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 31<-

[Albertus Ma]gnus <note>original: "gnus"; completing the name from the previous page</note>, Aristotle the Chemist in the book *Of the Perfect Mastery*, Rhazes, and Polydorus: for all these writers, as many as they are, err, or out of envy they write every kind of falsehood and propose received [errors], because they were also ignorant of the truth. Arsenic contains within itself three natural spirits: the first is volatile, combustible, corrosive, and penetrating to all metals. This spirit whitens **Venus** <note>original: "venerem"; the alchemical name for copper</note> and after several days renders it spongy. This craftsmanship belongs only to those who practice the caustic art <term>caustic art: the medical or chemical use of substances that burn or corrode living tissue</term>.

The second spirit is crystalline and sweet. The third is the **tinging spirit** <term>tinging spirit: the essence or "dye" capable of permanently changing the color and quality of a metal</term>, separated from the others mentioned above. True philosophers seek these three natural properties in arsenic for the perfect **projection** <term>projection: the final step in alchemy where the "medicine" or Stone is cast upon base metal to transform it</term> of the wise.

**Barber-surgeons** <note>original: "Barbitofores"; historical practitioners who performed both hair-cutting and minor surgeries</note>, however, practicing surgery, experience the sweet and crystalline nature (separated from the tinging spirit) for use in the treatment of wounds, and for buboes, carbuncles, anthrax <note>original: "antraces"; likely referring to malignant skin ulcers or pustules</note>, and other such defects of ulcers, which must be cured only by a mild artifice. 

Furthermore, that tinging spirit—unless its pure part is separated from its impure, the fixed from the volatile, and the secret tincture from the combustible—will by no means successfully achieve projection upon **mercury**, **Venus**, or any other imperfect metal according to one’s desire. All philosophers have hidden this secret as a most excellent mystery. Such a tinging spirit,

->32 AURORA<-

[the tinging spirit] <note>Context continued from previous page regarding the third spirit of arsenic.</note>, separated from the other two, as stated above. You shall join it to the spirit of **the moon** <note>original: "lunæ"; silver</note>, and at the same time allow them to digest for the space of thirty-two days, until they have put on a new body. After it has been ignited into inflammation by the heat of the sun on the fortieth natural day, the spirit appears with a bright whiteness, endowed with the perfect secret of **tinging** <term>tinging: the alchemical power to permanently change the color and nature of a substance, usually into gold or silver</term>. Finally, it is fit for **projection** <term>projection: the final stage of the Great Work where the alchemical catalyst is cast upon base metals to transform them</term>, namely, one part of it upon sixteen parts of an imperfect body, according to the sharpness of the preparation. From this appears shining and most excellent silver, as if it were dug out from the bowels of the earth.

### Chapter Twelve.

### Concerning the secret of Vitriol, and the red tincture to be drawn forth from it.

**V**itriol <note>A term for various sulfate minerals, such as copper sulfate (blue vitriol) or iron sulfate (green vitriol), prized for their acidic properties.</note>, a most noble mineral among the rest, has always been held in the highest esteem by the philosophers, because God the Most High has adorned it with wondrous gifts above all others. They veiled its secret in figures and enigmas, such as this: *You shall visit the interior of the earth; by rectifying, you shall find the hidden stone, the true medicine.* <note>This is the famous alchemical acronym V.I.T.R.I.O.L.: "Visita Interiora Terrae Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem."</note> By "the earth," they understood vitriol itself, and by "the interior of the earth," its sweetness and redness. For in the secret part of vitriol, a subtle, noble, and most fragrant juice, and a pure oil, lie hidden.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of vitriol from Chapter Twelve, specifically focusing on the delicate process of extracting its internal essence without destroying its natural "greenness."</meta>

->[OF THE] PHILOSOPHERS. 33<-

lies hidden. The method of drawing this forth must by no means be attempted through **calcination** <term>calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures in air to achieve decomposition or to remove volatile substances, often reducing it to a powder</term> or **distillation** <term>distillation: the action of purifying a liquid by a process of heating and cooling</term>. For indeed, it must not be deprived of its **greenness** <note>original: "viriditate"; in alchemy, this refers to the "living" power or life force of a substance</note>; for as soon as it is stripped of this, it must necessarily lack both its **secret** <note>original: "arcano"; the internal, curative, or transformative power of a substance</note> and its powers. It must be noted by everyone in this place that not only minerals, but even vegetables themselves, and similar things that show a green color on the outside, contain within them a red oil like blood, which is their secret. From this it is clear that the distillations of the **apothecaries** <note>original: "pharmacopeorum"; historical pharmacists who prepared and sold medicinal drugs</note> are void, vain, foolish, and of no importance, because they do not know how to draw forth the blood-red redness of vegetables. 

Wise nature herself turns the waters of all vegetables into a yellow color, and after that, into a most red oil like blood. That this process happens too slowly is chiefly caused by the excessive haste of ignorant distilling workers, by which the greenness is consumed. They have not learned to strengthen nature with its own powers, by which that noble greenness ought to be **rectified** <term>rectified: purified or refined through a process of repeated distillation or digestion</term> into redness by its own accord. 

White wine is an example, digesting itself into a yellow color, and in the course of time, the green color of the grape cluster turns by itself into a red color that was hidden under the blue. Therefore, if the greenness of vegetables and minerals is lost through the ignorance of the workers, the essence as well as the spirit of the oil and the most noble **balsam** <note>a fragrant, resinous substance used for healing; in alchemy, it refers to the preservative essence of a body</note> of the secrets will perish.

### Chapter Thirteen.
## Concerning the process of Vitriol toward the red tincture.

Vitriol <term>vitriol: a group of sulfate minerals, likely iron(II) sulfate here, prized for its ability to change colors during chemical operations</term> contains within itself many muddy and sticky imperfections; for which reason its watery greenness must be drawn out and **rectified** <term>rectified: purified or refined through repeated distillation or processing</term> quite often, until it sheds all the impurities of the earth. When all these rectifications are finished, one must take the greatest care that the material is not exposed to the sun, because the sun turns its greenness into paleness, and the **arcanum** <note>original: "arcano"; the hidden, essential power or medicinal secret of the substance</note> is absorbed at the same time. Let it be kept covered in a warm **heated room** <note>original: "hypocausto"; a room or chamber kept at a constant warmth, similar to a modern drying oven or an ancient Roman heated floor</note>, so that it does not become fouled by dust. Afterwards, let it be **digested** <term>digested: a process of gentle, continuous heating to mature a substance</term> in a closed glass for a period of several months, and for such a length of time until various colors appear, and finally the supreme redness. 

Even then, in this process, the redness must not be considered sufficiently **fixed** <term>fixed: the state where a substance is made stable and non-volatile, so it no longer evaporates when heated</term>; rather, it must be further purged from internal and accidental impurities of the earth in this manner. It must be rectified with vinegar until the earthy pollution is completely taken away and the dregs removed. This is the true and best rectification of its tincture, from which the **blessed oil** is to be drawn. From this, having been enclosed more carefully in glass, with an **alembic** <term>alembic: the top part of a still, used to catch and cool vapors</term> placed over it immediately, and the joints **sealed with bitumen** <note>original: "bituminatis commissuris"; using a sticky mineral pitch to make the apparatus airtight</note> so that the spirit does not escape, the spirit of that oil must be drawn forth with a sweet and slow fire. This oil is more delightful and sweeter than any **apothecary’s** <note>original: "pharmacopolico"; referring to the prepared medicines of a professional pharmacist or druggist</note> balsam or aromatic, and is entirely free from all sharpness. At the bottom of the **cucurbit** <term>cucurbit: the gourd-shaped lower vessel of a still that holds the liquid to be heated</term>, there will remain a certain very white earth, shining and glowing...

<summary>This page outlines the alchemical purification of vitriol, warning that sunlight destroys its potency and describing how to produce a sweet-smelling, blood-red oil through long-term digestion and distillation.</summary>
<keywords>vitriol, rectification, arcanum, digestion, red tincture, fixed oil, distillation, alchemy</keywords>

and like snow; keep and protect this from all dust. This very earth is completely separated from its redness. From there follows the greatest **arcanum** <note>original: "arcanum"; a deep secret or mystery of nature</note>, namely the super-celestial marriage of the soul—which has been highly purified and washed through the blood of the lamb—with its own shining, bright, and purified body. This is the true super-celestial marriage, which is prolonged into the final and destined day.

In this manner, therefore, the soul and the spirit of vitriol (which exist as its blood) are joined with its purified body, so that they might be inseparable for eternity. For this reason, take this our **foliated earth** <term>foliated earth: "terra foliata," the purified solid residue or salt that has reached a flaky, leaf-like consistency</term> in a glass vial, into which its oil should be gradually poured. The body will receive and embrace its soul in a moment. 

Since the body is very much moved by a desire for the soul, and the soul is most perfectly delighted by the embrace of the body. Keep a conjunction of this kind, placed in a **furnace of secrets** <note>original: "arcanorum furnum"; a specialized alchemical furnace designed to maintain specific, constant temperatures</note>, for forty days. When these have passed, you will have a most absolute oil of wonderful perfection, by which mercury and any other imperfect metals are turned into gold.

Now, let us apply the mind to **multiplication** <term>multiplication: the stage of the Great Work where the power and quantity of the alchemical agent (the Stone or Oil) is increased</term>. Take two parts of corporal mercury, over which you pour three parts (by the same weight) of the aforementioned oil, and let them remain together for forty days. By this proportion of weight and in this order, multiplication is achieved unto infinity.

**Chapter fourteen,**
*Concerning the secrets and mysteries of antimony, for the red*
*tincture, for transmutation.*

**A**ntimony is the true bath of gold. Philosophers, however, have called it the "examiner" and **Stilange** <note>original: "Stilangem"; likely a corruption of "stibium" (antimony) or a specific alchemical name for the substance's purifying quality</note>. Poets, indeed, say that in this bath, Vulcan washed **Phoebus** <note>In alchemical allegory, Phoebus (the Sun) represents Gold, and Vulcan represents the purifying fire</note> and purged him of all filth and imperfections. It is born from the purest and most noble mercury and sulfur, of the genus of vitriol, in metallic form and splendor. Some philosophers have called it the "white lead of the wise," or simply lead. 

Therefore, take as much antimony as you wish—and let it be of the very best quality—let this be dissolved in its own **strong water** <term>strong water: "aqua fortis," typically nitric acid used to dissolve metals</term>, and then cast into cold water. Add a little **Saffron of Mars** <term>Saffron of Mars: "crocus Martius," a red powder made of iron oxide, used here to help precipitate the mixture</term>, so that it falls as a sediment to the bottom of the vessel; for otherwise, it does not cast off its dregs. 

After it has been dissolved in this manner, it will have acquired the highest beauty. Let it be placed in a glass vessel fortified all around with very compact **lute** <term>lute: "luto," a paste of clay or sand used to seal vessels and protect them from direct heat</term>, or in a stone flask, and let there be mixed in **tutia** <term>tutia: also known as tutty, a crude zinc oxide collected from the flues of smelting furnaces</term> that has been calcined and sublimated, subjected to a perfect degree of fire. One must be very careful to avoid liquefaction, because the excessive heat can break the glass. From one pound of this antimony, a **sublimation** <note>the process of turning a solid into vapor and back to solid to purify it</note> is obtained, perfected in the space of two days. This sublimate, having been placed in a phial, so that by a third part the water touches...

Let the spirit be drawn off several times, and let the gold be dissolved again just as many times. Let the final solution be kept with the spirit of wine and be circulated <note>circulation: a process of continuous distillation and condensation within a closed vessel, allowing the substances to "mature"</note> for a month. After this, let the volatile gold and the spirit of wine be distilled through an alembic three or four times, so that it flows down into the receiving vessel and is brought to its highest essence.

### Chapter Fifteen.
### Concerning the projection to be made through the mystery and secret of antimony.

No exact weight can be assigned in this work of projection <term>projection: the final step of the alchemical Great Work, where the medicinal "tincture" is cast upon molten base metal to transform it into gold or silver</term>, although the tincture itself is drawn from some subject in a certain proportion and with the proper instruments. For indeed, this medicine sometimes tints thirty, forty, and sometimes sixty, eighty, or even a hundred parts of imperfect metal. Thus, the core of this business revolves especially around the purification of the medicine and the industry <note>meaning the skill and diligence</note> of the operator, as well as the greater purity of one [metal] than the other...

...the cleanliness and purity of the imperfect body taken in hand. This alone is worthy of note: if it happens that the operator has used too much of the tincture, he can correct this error by adding more of the imperfect metal. If, however, there is too much of the subject <note>the base metal being transformed</note>, such that the powers of the tincture are weakened, this defect is easily removed by cupellation <term>cupellation: "cineritio," a refining process where metal is heated in a cupel (a porous bowl of bone ash) to separate precious metals from lead and impurities</term>, or by cementations, or by washings with raw antimony. There is nothing in this part that should delay or hinder the worker; let him only consider what has been omitted by the philosophers and studiously veiled by some: namely, that in projections, the revivification of the imperfect bodies is necessary. This is called animation, or, so to speak, "spiritualization," about which some have said that their metals are not common ones, because they live and have a soul.

->*Animation is performed in this manner.*<-

Take copper <note>original: "Veneris"</note> beaten into thin plates, as much as you wish—ten, twenty, or forty pounds—and let them be encrusted with pastes made from arsenic and calcined tartar <term>tartar: "tartaro calcinato," a salty crust found in wine barrels, used here as a chemical reagent</term>, and let them be calcined in their own vessel for the space of twenty-four hours. Finally, the copper, crushed into powder, should be washed and cleaned thoroughly. Let the calcination be repeated with washing, three or four—

### Chapter Sixteen.
### *On the universal matter of the philosophers' stone.*

After the mortification <term>mortification: the chemical "death" or breakdown of a substance, often involving a change in color to black (nigredo)</term> of vegetable substances, through the joining of two minerals—namely, sulfur and salt—they are transmuted into a mineral nature, so that from there, at last, perfect minerals result. For indeed, in the mineral tunnels and broad fields of the earth, vegetables are found...

From this mineral condition, a certain perfect metallic essence sometimes arises, and this occurs by a progression from one degree into another. 

But let us return to the Philosophers' Stone, the matter of which (as some have mentioned) is, above all things, the most difficult to find and the most obscure to the intellect. The method of investigating this Stone—as with any other thing—and the most certain rule for determining what they contain and what they are worth, is the diligent examination of their root and seed <note>original: "spermatis"; in alchemy, the "seed" refers to the generative essence or potential within a substance</note>, through which one arrives at true knowledge. Toward this end, the consideration of first principles is extremely necessary: specifically, by what path or by what means nature first reached the goal of perfection from a state of imperfection. 

For this consideration, it is most useful to have it well-established that all things created by nature consist of three first principles—namely, natural Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt <term>Tria Prima: the three "prime" substances (Mercury/Spirit, Sulfur/Soul, Salt/Body) that Paracelsian alchemy held as the components of all matter</term>—mixed together into one, such that in some things they are volatile <note>original: "volatilia"; substances that evaporate or change easily when heated</note>, while in others they are fixed <note>original: "fixa"; substances that resist the fire and remain stable</note>. It remains, however, volatile at this stage, and in scarcely a hundred years is it cooked into a metal. From this arose the common saying—that **mercury** and **sulfur** are the "matter" of metals—as is also evident from the reports of mineral miners. 

Yet, it is not common mercury, nor common sulfur, that are the matter of metals. These are incorporated and innate within the perfected metals and their forms, so that they never flee from the fire, nor are they corrupted by the destructive force of the elements. Certainly, through the dissolution of such a natural mixture, our mercury is "tamed" <note>original: "domitus"; a common alchemical metaphor for fixing a volatile substance so it no longer evaporates under heat</note>, as all philosophers say. This is what **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of Hermeticism and alchemy.</note> asserts in these words: "The **Sun**," he says, "and the **Moon** are the roots of this art." This same point is confirmed by the fact that like always brings forth and generates its own like. And just as we know there are two stones—the **white** and the **red** <note>The "White Stone" or Tincture transforms metals into Silver, while the "Red Stone" transforms them into Gold.</note>—so too are there two materials of the Stone: sun and moon joined together in their own marriage, both natural and artificial. And just as we see that a man and a woman are completely unable to generate without the seed of both, in the same way our **man (the sun)** and his **woman (the moon)** cannot conceive without the seed and sperm of each, nor can they strive toward any generation.

From this, the philosophers gathered that a third thing is necessary: namely, the living seed of both the man and the woman. Without this, they judged their whole work to be useless and empty. This kind of sperm is **mercury**, which, through the natural conjunction of both bodies—sun and moon—receives their nature into itself in union. Only then, and not before, is the work fit for the meeting, the entrance, and the generation <note>The text uses sexual metaphors—*congressum* and *ingressum*—to describe the chemical interaction and penetration of the substances.</note>, through the male and female force and power. Their sayings, which we mentioned before, are truly golden and must always be held in the highest esteem because they contain nothing superfluous and nothing invalid.

In summary, therefore, the **matter of the philosophers' stone**...

### Chapter Seventeen.
->On the preparation of the matter of the philosophers' stone.<-

Nature requires this above all: namely, that its "philosophical man" be brought into a mercurial substance, so that it may be born into the **philosophers' stone**. Furthermore, it must be noted that those common preparations of **Geber**, **Albertus Magnus**, **Thomas Aquinas**, **Rupescissa** <note>Johannes de Rupescissa, a 14th-century alchemist known for his works on "quintessence" and medical alchemy.</note>, **Polydorus**, and those like them, are nothing other than "particular" <note>In alchemy, a "particular" preparation is one that works only on specific metals or for limited goals, rather than the "Universal" goal of the Great Work.</note> solutions, sublimations, and calcinations. These pertain very little to our "Universal" work, which requires only the most secret fire of the philosophers. 

Therefore, let **fire** and **Azoth** <note>original: "azoth"; a term for the universal solvent or the essential mercurial spirit. The word was formed from the first and last letters of the Latin, Greek, and Hebrew alphabets (A-Z, Alpha-Omega, Aleph-Tav) to symbolize the beginning and the end of all things.</note> suffice for you. Although the philosophers make mention of some preparations...

<summary>This page defines the "Mercury of the Philosophers" as a perfected, fiery substance that unites celestial and terrestrial powers, while dismissing common laboratory techniques in favor of a "secret fire" and "Azoth."</summary>
<keywords>Mercury of the Philosophers, hermaphrodite Adam, Hermes Trismegistus, orphan stone, whiteness and redness, Chapter Seventeen, Azoth, universal preparation</keywords>

--- Page 47 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the preparation of the philosophical matter, specifically clarifying that the various alchemical stages are part of a single natural process rather than separate manual tasks.</meta>

->46 AURORA<-

...tion, such as **putrefaction**, **distillation**, **sublimation**, **calcination**, **coagulation**, **whitening**, **reddening**, **waxing**, and **fixation**, etc. <note>These terms represent the "Twelve Gates" or stages of the alchemical process. Putrefaction is the decay of the matter; calcination is its reduction to ash; fixation is making a volatile substance stable under heat.</note> You ought to understand this very thing: that in their universal sense, nature itself fulfills all these operations within the said matter, and not the operator—provided it is in the philosophical vessel and by a fire similar [to nature's], and not a common fire. The **white** and the **red** spring forth from a single root without any intermediary. It is dissolved by itself, and joined by itself; it turns white and turns red; it becomes saffron-colored and black by its own power; it marries itself and conceives within itself. It must, therefore, be boiled, roasted, and melted; it ascends and it descends. Indeed, all these operations are but one single operation, performed by fire alone.

Nevertheless, some philosophers have dissolved the "body of the sun" <note>original: "corpus solis"; in alchemy, the "Sun" is the symbol for gold.</note> through a highly graduated essence of wine <note>High-proof alcohol or "spirit of wine," often used as a solvent in early chemistry.</note> and rendered it volatile so that it might ascend through the alembic <note>A distillation apparatus consisting of two retorts connected by a tube.</note>, thinking this to be the true volatile matter of the philosophers, when it is not. And even if it is a secret <note>original: "arcanum"; a hidden mystery or a potent medicinal/chemical preparation.</note> not to be despised—to bring this perfect metallic body into a volatile and spiritual substance—nevertheless they err in the separation of the elements. For the process of the monks—namely **Lully**, **Richard the Englishman**, **Rupescissa**, and others—is erroneous. <note>Raymond Lull (c. 1232–1315), Richardus Anglicus (13th century), and Jean de Roquetaillade (Rupescissa, c. 1310–1366) were famous medieval authors of alchemical and medical texts who focused heavily on the "quintessence" and distillation.</note> By this process, they believed they could separate gold into a subtle, spiritual, and elemental power, each part individually, and afterwards, through circulation...

<summary>The author argues that the Great Work is a single natural process driven by nature rather than the alchemist’s manual interference, and critiques famous medieval alchemists for incorrectly attempting to separate gold's elements through spirits of wine.</summary>
