<meta>This page continues the argument that true alchemical transformation is a natural, internal process. It critiques alchemists who attempt to force the separation of elements using external laboratory tools, contrasting their failure with the perfection of Nature's own methods.</meta>

->OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 47<-

...separation and <term>rectification</term> <note>The process of purifying a liquid through repeated distillation to reach its most potent form.</note> to couple together again into one, but in vain. Indeed, even if one element is in some way separated from another, nevertheless any element separated in this manner can be separated again into another element. These parts afterwards, by means of "pelican-like" circulation <note>The "Pelican" is a specialized alchemical vessel with two curved arms that return distilled vapors back into the base, allowing for a continuous cycle of evaporation and condensation.</note> or distillation, can by no means return into one again; instead, they always remain as a certain volatile matter and <term>potable gold</term> <note>original: "aurum potabile"; a medicinal form of gold dissolved into a liquid state, long sought by alchemists as a universal cure.</note>, as they call it. 

The reason why they could not achieve their intention is this: because nature does not wish to be pulled apart or separated in this way by human disjunctions, such as through earthly glassware and instruments. She alone knows her own operations and the weights of the elements, whose separations, rectifications, and couplings she carries out without the aid of any operator or manual artifice—provided the matter is contained in the <term>secret fire</term> <note>In alchemy, the "secret fire" often refers to an internal, non-combustive heat or a specific chemical catalyst, rather than a literal flame.</note> and in its own hidden vessel. 

Therefore, the separation of elements is impossible by man; although it may appear to happen, it is not true, regardless of what is said of **Raymond Lull** <note>Raymond Lull (c. 1232–1315) was a philosopher to whom many alchemical texts were later attributed. Legend claims he transmuted gold for the King of England.</note> and his English "noble of gold," <note>A reference to the "Rose Noble" coin, which legend claims Lull manufactured for King Edward through alchemy to fund a crusade.</note> which he is falsely thought to have manufactured. For Nature herself has her own separator within her, who joins again what he separates without human help; she knows best of all the proportion of each element—not man, whatever erroneous writers [may claim]...

->48 AURORA<-

...according to their frivolous and false recipes, let them babble about this "volatile gold" <note>original: "volatili hoc auro"; likely a reference to a chemical preparation that lacked the stability of true gold.</note> of theirs. This is the opinion of the philosophers: that when they have placed their matter into the <term>secret fire</term> <note>A term for the internal, self-sustaining heat of the alchemical process, rather than a literal flame.</note>, it is nurtured on all sides by their moderate philosophical heat, so that, as it begins to pass through corruption, it turns black. This operation they call <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The stage where the substance is broken down; it was believed that life or transformation could only emerge from the decay of the old form.</note>, and they name this blackness the **crow’s head** <note>original: "caput corvi"; the traditional name for the *nigredo* or "blackening" stage, symbolizing the death of the old material.</note>. 

They describe the ascent and descent of this matter as <term>distillation</term>, <term>ascension</term>, and <term>descension</term>. They say that its drying out is **coagulation**, and its whitening is **calcination**. And because it becomes fluid and soft through heat, they make mention of **ceration** <note>The process of bringing a substance to a wax-like consistency (from the Latin *cera*, meaning wax).</note>. When it ceases to ascend and remains liquid at the bottom, they say that **fixation** is present. In this manner, therefore, the names of the philosophical operations are to be understood, and not otherwise.

...for the **whitening of its earth** <note>original: *dealbationis suæ terræ*; the *albedo* stage where the matter becomes pure and white.</note>, a glass vessel, and in the third stage for **fixation** <note>The process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire.</note>, an earthenware vessel. For invisible celestial influences and the impressions of the stars are especially necessary for the work; otherwise it would be impossible to complete the unsurpassable **Oriental, Persian, Chaldean, and Egyptian stone** <note>The author attributes the Stone to the great ancient civilizations known for their astronomical and mystical knowledge.</note>, through which **Anaxagoras** <note>A Greek philosopher (c. 500–428 BCE) who famously claimed the sun was a hot stone and is said to have predicted the fall of a meteorite.</note> recognized the powers of the entire firmament and foretold that a great stone would descend from heaven to earth—which indeed occurred after his death. 

To the **Cabalists** <note>Mystics who study the hidden, divine structure of the universe.</note>, especially, our vessel is known; it must be manufactured according to true geometric proportion and measure, and from a certain **squaring of the circle** <note>A symbolic reference to balancing the elements or finding the divine center within a physical form.</note>, so that the spirit and soul of our matter, once separated from its body, may be able to elevate this with themselves toward the height of heaven. If the vessel is narrower, wider, taller, or lower than is right, or than the dominant and working spirits and soul desire, the heat of our **secret philosophical fire** (which is indeed most sharp) will urge the violently excited matter to excessive activity, so that the vessel shatters into a thousand pieces.

The operator should also most accurately note exactly what the matter releases and rejects from itself in the first solution. The method of describing the form of the vessel is difficult: it must be such as nature itself requires, sought and investigated from one thing and another, so that from the **height of the philosophical heaven**, elevated from the **philosophical earth** <note>In alchemy, "heaven" refers to the top of the vessel where vapors (spirits) collect, and "earth" refers to the solid precipitate at the bottom.</note>, it may be able to work into the fruit of its terrestrial body. 

It must certainly have this form so that the **separation and purification of the elements** <note>The process of breaking a substance down into its constituent parts—traditionally Fire, Air, Water, and Earth—to be later recombined in a perfected state.</note> can occur when the fire drives one from another, and so that each may be able to occupy the place in which it belongs, and the **Sun and other planets** <note>The "Sun" (Gold) and "planets" (other metals like Silver, Mercury, Lead, etc.) represent the various metallic spirits or qualities circulating within the vessel.</note> may exercise their operations around the elemental earth, and their course in the circuit may not be hindered or driven by too rapid a motion. According to all these things which have been said, it is necessary that it has the correct proportion of roundness and height.

Instruments, indeed, for the first purification of mineral bodies are melting vessels, bellows, tongs, cup- <note>The text breaks at *capel-*, likely *capellae* (cupels), which are shallow porous bowls used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals.</note>

### Chapter Nineteen.
### On the secret fire of the philosophers.

<note>The chapter begins with a large, decorative initial letter C.</note>

**C**elebrated is this maxim of the philosophers: **"Fire and Azoth are sufficient for you."** <note>original: *Ignis, & Azoc tibi sufficiant*; Azoth was considered the "universal solvent" or the hidden spirit within matter.</note> For fire alone is the whole work and the entire art. Furthermore, as many as build up their fire from coals are in error, keeping the vessel in that heat. Some have attempted the work with the heat of horse-dung, but in vain. With the fire of coals, without any intermediary, they have **sublimed** <note>Sublimation is the process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again.</note> the matter, but they have not **dissolved** it. Others have raised the heat with their lamps, asserting that this is the secret fire of the philosophers for fabricating their Stone. Some have placed it in a bath, first in mounds of ant eggs; others in juniper ashes; some have sought the fire in **quicklime**, <note>Calcium oxide, which generates intense heat when mixed with water.</note> in tartar, vitriol, nitre, and so on. Others seek it in **burning water**, <note>original: *aqua ardente*; a term for concentrated alcohol or ethanol, which is flammable.</note> just as **Thomas Aquinas** <note>A reference to alchemical works falsely attributed to the medieval theologian Thomas Aquinas.</note> speaks falsely concerning this fire, saying that even God and his angels cannot be without this fire, but use it daily. What blasphemy is this? Is it not a most manifest lie that God cannot be without the elemental fire of "burning water"? All heats excited by these means which have been mentioned are utterly useless for our work. See that you...

### Chapter Twenty.
### On the ferment of the philosophers, and weight.

The philosophers have labored greatly in the art of **ferments** <note>In alchemy, a "ferment" is a substance added to the "prime matter" to give it a specific form or quality, analogous to how leaven or yeast transforms dough into bread.</note> and **fermentations**, which seems the most important among other matters. Concerning this, some have even made a vow to God and the philosophers that they would never reveal the manifest secret of this thing through likenesses or parables. Yet since **Hermes**, <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure considered the "father" of alchemy and author of the *Emerald Tablet*.</note> the father of all philosophers, in the book of his *Seven Treatises*, most clearly reveals the ferments, saying: that they consist of nothing but their own **paste**. <note>original: *pasta*; a semi-solid mixture of alchemical materials.</note> He says further that the ferment whitens the composition, prevents burning, and utterly retards the flow of the **tincture**, <note>The "tincture" is the potent liquid or essence capable of transmuting metals.</note> strengthens the bodies, and expands their union. He also says that this is the key and the end of the work, concluding that the ferment is nothing else but a paste; as for the **Sun**, <note>The Sun (Sol) is the alchemical symbol for Gold.</note> it is nothing but the Sun, and for the **Moon**, <note>The Moon (Luna) is the alchemical symbol for Silver.</note> nothing other than the Moon. Others affirm that the ferment is the **soul**, which accomplishes nothing unless it be properly prepared by the **magistery**. <note>The "magistery" refers to the entire technical process or the mastery required to produce the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

Some zealots of this art seek the art in common sulfur, arsenic, **tutty**, <note>A crude form of zinc oxide collected from the flues of smelting furnaces.</note> **orpiment**, <note>A bright yellow arsenic sulfide mineral used as a pigment.</note> vitriol, and so on—but in vain. Because the substance which is sought is the same as that from which it must be drawn.

D 3

<meta>This page continues the discussion of alchemical fermentation from the previous page and introduces the concept of "weight" (proportions), distinguishing between natural and artificial weights of the elements.</meta>

54 AURORA

[It must be realized] therefore, that fermentations of this kind, according to the vow of the zealous, <note>The "zealous" refers to dedicated alchemists who have taken oaths of secrecy.</note> do not succeed as they would wish, but as is clear from what was said above, only through natural successes. To come at last to **weight**, <note>original: *pondus*; in alchemy, this refers to the precise proportions or balance of ingredients rather than just heavy mass.</note> this must be observed in two ways: the first is natural, and the second is artificial.

The natural weight achieves its effect in the earth, through nature and "concordance," <note>A harmonious agreement or alignment between the different substances used.</note> about which **Arnaldus** <note>Likely Arnaldus de Villa Nova, a 13th-century physician and one of the most cited authorities in medieval alchemy.</note> writes. If more or less is added to the earth than nature allows, the soul is suffocated, no fruit is perceived, and no **fixation** <note>The process by which a volatile substance is made stable and resistant to fire.</note> occurs. The same must be thought regarding water; if more or less of it is taken, it brings about no less inconvenient damage: its excess makes the matter excessively moist, and its lack makes it drier and harder than it should be.

If too much air is present, too much is impressed upon the **tincture**; <note>The "tincture" is the spiritual essence or pigment capable of transforming base metals into gold.</note> if too little, the body will turn out pale. Likewise, if the fire is too intense, the matter is burned; if too weak, it has no power to dry, nor to dissolve, nor to heat the other elements: in these things consists the elemental weight.

But the **artificial weight** is most hidden, since it is contained within the magical art of proportions. Between spirit, soul, and body, the philosophers say that weight consists of **sulfur**, as the ruler of the work; for indeed the soul desires sulfur above all, and necessarily observes it by reason of weight. Understand it in this manner: Our matter is united to the **red sulfur**

->OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 55<-

...of fixed sulfur, <note>original: *sulfuri fixo*; in alchemy, sulfur represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle. When "fixed," it has been made stable and resistant to the transformative power of fire.</note> to which the third part of the **governance** <note>original: *regiminis*; the "regimen" is the specific sequence of operations and the careful management of heat required during the work.</note> is entrusted, up to the final degree, so that it may complete the operation of the **stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and providing an elixir of life.</note> to infinity, and persist with it, together with its fire; and it should consist of equal weight with the matter itself, in all things and through all things, without any variation of any degree of change.

Therefore, after the matter has been adapted and mixed in its proportioned weight, it must be perfectly closed in the **vessel of the philosophers** <note>original: *vase philosophorum*; a specialized glass container, often egg-shaped, designed to withstand long periods of heat.</note> and with its own seal, and committed to the **secret fire**, <note>original: *igni secreto*; a steady, internal, or philosophical heat that is often distinguished from common culinary fire.</note> in which the **philosophical sun** <note>original: *sol philosophicus*; a metaphor for the gold of the wise or the completed Stone, which "shines" with spiritual and physical perfection.</note> will arise and ascend, and it will illuminate all things which have awaited its light and desired it with much hope.

With these few words, we wish the secret of the stone to be concluded, lacking in no point and not defective, for which we give immortal thanks to God. Now we shall unlock our Treasure for you, which cannot be paid for by the wealth of the whole world.

56 THE TREASURE

### The Treasure of Treasures of THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS.

**N**ature has produced one admirable kind of mineral in the bowels of the earth, and that of two types: this is found in various places in Europe. The best, which was placed into my hands and confirmed by experiment, is the **star** <note>original: *astrum*; in Paracelsian thought, an "astrum" is the celestial or spiritual virtue of a substance, its "inner star" or essential power.</note> of the sphere of the **sun**, <note>original: *spherae solis*; referring to the macrocosmic influence of the Sun, associated with gold and the highest perfection.</note> according to the shape of the **greater world** <note>original: *maioris mundi*; the Macrocosm or the universe at large, as opposed to the Microcosm (man).</note> from the beginning. The other consists in the **southern star**; in its first flowering, it is the **viscous slime of the earth**, <note>original: *viscus est terrae*; a thick, sticky substance considered the primal "glue" or base matter from which minerals grow.</note> brought into the light by its own star, and adorned with **redness** in its first coagulation. In it, all the flowers and colors of minerals are included, concerning which much is found among the philosophers.

As far as it pertains to its cold and moist nature, it has been adapted to **water**, by a certain comparison. However, the perfect knowledge of it, when brought to experience, has remained hidden from most—indeed, almost all philosophers—as many as have hitherto shot their arrows at the target before me. Striving in vain, they have wandered very widely from the true and complete mark. 

They were of the opinion that **mercury** and **sulfur** <note>The traditional two-principle theory of metals (Mercury-Sulfur theory) which Paracelsus famously expanded.</note> are the mother and father of all metals, but truly, they did not remember the **third**. <note>Paracelsus is referring to **Salt**, the third of his "Tria Prima" (Three Primes: Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt). This was his revolutionary addition to alchemical theory.</note> Indeed, what is more, none of them has yet been able to take into their hands the **fishing net** <note>An allegorical term for the specific chemical method or apparatus used to "catch" or extract the essential spirit from the matter.</note> which belongs to the **true water**, the mother of metals, which indeed...

<summary>Paracelsus introduces the "Treasure of Treasures," identifying a specific mineral matter influenced by the sun and stars as the true source of metals. He critiques previous philosophers for only recognizing Mercury and Sulfur while ignoring the "third" principle (Salt).</summary>

<keywords>Paracelsus, Treasure of Treasures, mineral nature, macrocosm, Tria Prima, sulfur and mercury, viscous earth, mother of metals</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Paracelsus's "The Treasure of Treasures," specifically expanding on the nature of the "one admirable mineral" mentioned on page 56. He contrasts his chemical (spagyric) methods with the traditional medical schools of Galen and Avicenna, using the metaphor of a tree to explain mineral maturation.</meta>

OF THE PHILOSOPHERS, 57

indeed, when separated by the **spagyric art**, <note>original: *artificio spagirico*; "spagyric" refers to the alchemical process of separating a substance into its components and then recombining them in a purified state.</note> it reveals its "fish," which neither the fishing of **Galen** nor the torn net of **Avicenna** <note>Galen and Avicenna were the foundational authorities of medieval medicine; Paracelsus famously rejected their reliance on herbal "humors" in favor of chemical and mineral cures.</note> could ever catch. If I had to demonstrate to modern physicians even just the meaning of **conjunction**, **solution**, and **coagulation**—which nature itself revealed and demonstrated in this creation from the beginning of the world—scarcely a year, nor all the paper in existence, would suffice to instruct them. I say, indeed, that in this mineral three substances are contained: namely, **Mercury**, **Sulphur**, and **mineral water**, <note>original: *mineralem aquam*; in later Paracelsian theory, this "water" or "balsam" is often associated with "Salt," completing the *Tria Prima* (the Three Primes of Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt).</note> from which it is composed. When separated by the spagyric art, it is dissolved in its own immature liquor, just as a pear is hidden within its tree. 

The tree is the "hider" of the pear; within it, as the stars and nature come together, it puts forth green branches, and later in March reveals tiny buds and flowers, progressing toward the production of the fruit and the pear itself, until finally the fruit emerges in autumn. In no different a manner should we think of the minerals in the bowels of the earth, which are pushed forth by the **stars**: <note>original: *per astra*; Paracelsus believed that minerals "grew" in the earth like plants, fed by celestial influences or "stars" from the heavens.</note> this must be considered most highly by the alchemist who pants after nature’s treasure. The method of this art—its beginning, middle, and end—I shall reveal in the following treatise, together with its water, sulphur, and **balsam**. <note>original: *balsamo*; a preservative life-force often identified with the Salt or the internal "mummy" of a substance.</note> By the solution of these three, and their subsequent conjunction into one, the whole business of the work is completed.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for processing the "admirable mineral" (cinnabar) mentioned on the previous page. The text begins mid-sentence, concluding the previous thought before launching into a detailed spagyric procedure involving distillation and calcination.</meta>

18 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**THE TREASURY**

...[it is seen] in this manner. Take the ore of **cinnabar**, which you should wash with **celestial water** <note>original: *aqua cœleſti*; this typically refers to rainwater, believed by alchemists to possess special vital properties</note> for three hours; finally strain it, and dissolve it in **aqua regia** <note>original: *aqua regis*; literally "king's water," a powerful acid capable of dissolving gold. Here, the author specifies a recipe made from vitriol, saltpeter, and common salt</note> made from vitriol, saltpeter, and common salt. Let it be drawn off through an **alembic** <note>a chemical apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube, used for distilling volatile substances</note>, poured back again, and treated by **cohobation** <note>the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid from the same solid matter to increase its potency</note>, so that the pure may be separated from the impure, which remains below. 

Let it putrefy in **horse dung** <note>a common alchemical method for providing a steady, gentle, and natural heat for fermentation or digestion</note> for a month, and afterwards let the elements be separated, so that when they have revealed their signs, they may be distilled through an alembic at the first degree of fire, by which **water** and **air** ascend first, and finally **fire**, and so on through the remaining degrees, which can be discerned by an experienced operator. At the bottom of the vessel, the **earth** remains, in which lies hidden all that has been sought by many, yet found by very few. 

You shall artificially **calcine** <note>to reduce a substance to powder or ash by heat</note> this earth enclosed in a **reverberatory furnace**, proceeding from the first to the fifth degree of heat, spending five hours at each degree. This is done so that you may have a **volatile salt**, as subtle as **alcohol** <note>original: *alcool*; in this period, the term referred to a fine, impalpable powder or the most refined essence of a substance, rather than specifically liquid ethanol</note>, and a constant **star** <note>original: *aſtrum*; in Paracelsian alchemy, the "star" refers to the inner celestial virtue or the quintessence of a substance</note> of fire and earth, which you shall separate by the elements of water and air that you saved earlier. 

Finally, place it in digestion in a **bain-marie** <note>original: *balnei maris*; a water bath used for gentle heating</note> for eight hours, and you will see what has hitherto been unknown to many alchemists and least considered. Separate it skillfully and in a **spagyric manner** <note>the process of separating a substance into its components and then recombining them in a purified state</note> according to your experience, and the earth itself will be rendered wonderfully white, from which the **tincture** <note>the concentrated medicinal or transformative essence</note> will have been extracted. Join the element of fire with the salt...

...of the earth, reduced to a fine powder <term>alcolizato</term> <note>original: *alcolizato*; likely derived from the Arabic *al-kuhl*, meaning a substance ground into an impalpable, subtle powder</note>, through digestion and the artifice of the **pelican** <note>original: *pellicanicum artificium*; a "pelican" is a circulatory distillation vessel with two side-arms that return the condensed vapors to the body, symbolizing the bird that feeds its young from its own breast</note>, this substance will still deposit another sediment, which you shall indeed separate from the pure. By this artifice, the **tincture of the sun** is perfected in its degree. Then at last, take twice as much of **Venus** <note>original: *veneris*; the alchemical name for copper</note> brought to its highest point and prepared in the **spagyric** manner <note>the process of separating, purifying, and recombining the "three primes" of a substance</note>, to which you should pour the elements of **water** and **air** that you had saved; dissolve and putrefy them for a month, as before. And when it has reached perfection, you will see the **sign of the elements**. Separate one from the other, since the secret **red** appears to the eye, coming out from the **white**. You shall separate this from the white, for it is a **red tincture**, so powerful that it stains any white bodies with redness, or red bodies into whiteness, which is greatly

# 80 ->TREASURE<-

It is truly marvelous. Drive this **tincture** through the **retort**: you will see blackness <note>original: *nigredinem*; referring to the "nigredo" or black stage of the alchemical work, representing putrefaction and the death of the old form</note> rise up, which you shall drive through the retort again, and do so quite often, until it becomes white <note>original: *albescat*; the "albedo" or white stage, signifying purification</note>. Follow through with this your work, and do not lose heart on account of the excessive labor. Rectify it so many times until you see the **green lion** <term>viridem leonem</term> <note>a potent mineral acid or a specific state of mercury/vitriol capable of "devouring" or dissolving gold</note>, true and clear, heavy and weighty, which stains into perfect gold. Do not desist from the work of which we now make mention, until you have the signs and see the **lion** and the **treasure**, a treasure not to be surpassed even by that of **Leo X** <note>Pope Leo X (reigned 1513–1521), famous for the immense wealth and splendor of the Medici papacy; the author suggests this alchemical "lion" is more valuable than the "Lion" Pope's riches</note>. May it be well for him who shall find it and knows how to apply it to the tincture. 

This is the true **balsam** <note>a preservative essence believed to prevent decay</note> of the celestial stars, which allows no body to pass into putrefaction, and leaves no leprosy, gout, or dropsy unexpelled, through the administration of a single grain, if it be fermented with the **sulfur of the sun** <note>original: *sulphure solis*; the "sulfur" is the active, soul-like principle of gold</note>. 

Oh, you **Charles the German** <note>Referring to Holy Roman Emperor Charles V (reigned 1519–1556)</note>, where do you have your treasure? Where are your doctors and physicians, where are the boilers of **guaiac wood** <note>original: *ligni guaiaci*; a wood imported from the Americas used as a treatment for syphilis, which the author here dismisses as a mere laxative</note>, who merely purge and loosen? Is it thus that your heaven is provoked, and your stars have deviated from the straight course, and your swampy line <note>original: *lineaq́; tua palustris*; perhaps a metaphor for a stagnant or muddy lineage or path</note> has turned aside into another way? What, have your eyes been transmuted into glass and carbuncle, so that they see only those things which pertain to ornament and superficial spectacle, to pomp and pride? Truly, if your Doctors knew that their Prince (whom they call **Galen** <note>Galen of Pergamon, the ancient physician whose "humoral" theories were the standard of the time. Alchemists like Paracelsus famously challenged his authority</note>) dwelt among the inhabitants of the **Underworld**, and that he had written back here from there, whither...

->OF THE PHILOSOPHERS. 61<-

...himself, so that he might reveal his own deserved condemnation openly to the whole world, they would brand themselves with a fox’s tail <note>original: *vulpina cauda*; in the 16th century, the fox's tail was a symbol of deceit, flattery, or folly; here, the author suggests his enemies are marked by their own absurdity</note> out of sheer astonishment. No less should be judged concerning **Avicenna** <note>Ibn Sina (c. 980–1037), a Persian physician whose *Canon of Medicine* was the supreme authority in medieval European universities; Paracelsus famously rejected his teachings in favor of chemical medicine</note>, who sits in the porch of Hell. With him I have held a dispute and controversy regarding his **potable gold** <term>aurum potabile</term> <note>a liquid preparation of gold thought to be a panacea or a life-extending medicine</note>, as well as his **philosopher’s stone** and his **theriac** <note>original: *teriacam*; a complex herbal jam used as a universal antidote, which the author dismisses here as ineffective "sophistry"</note>. 

Oh, you ghosts <note>original: *laruæ*; literally "masks" or "phantoms," implying these doctors are hollow imitations of real men</note> of the sophists <note>intellectual pretenders who use clever but false arguments to deceive</note>, who fabricate the medical art! Yet this art is born from God and entrusted to Nature—not to you most unworthy men, by whom it is undeservedly despised. See for yourselves, you impostors of men, who love the high chairs <note>original: *cathedras*; refers to the prestigious university chairs occupied by medical professors</note> of the earth upon which you sit: after my death, my disciples shall rise up. They will expose you as hypocrites and bring you into the light, along with your most filthy cooks <note>original: *coquis*; a derogatory term for the laboratory assistants or pharmacists who mindlessly prepared traditional recipes without understanding the chemical principles</note>, you who wretchedly seduce your Christian **Princes and Potentates** and lead them to death with your medicine. Woe to you on the final Day of Judgment! 

I hope that my **Monarchy** <note>original: *Monarchiam*; a term frequently used by Paracelsus to describe his sovereign and divinely-appointed authority over the true medical and chemical arts</note> will triumph with the honor due to me. Not because I extol myself, but because Nature herself extols me. For I was born a physician from her; I follow her, she knows me, and I know her. This is because I have seen the light that is within her. I have proved it in the form of the **Microcosm** <note>the human being, viewed as a miniature mirror of the entire universe; a central tenet of Paracelsian philosophy</note>, and I have found it to be so in her world, which is the truth. However, so that I may return to the undertaking and satisfy my disciples, to whom I am also most excellently imparting my doctrine...

By this way, you will have the tincture of the alchemists, the weight of which is by no means to be described in this place, but in the *Book of Transmutations*. Whoever possesses one ounce of the **star of the sun** <note>the "star" refers to the quintessence or the most potent, celestial virtue extracted from the metal</note>, and projects it over several ounces of gold, will tinge its own body. If he has the **star of mercury**, he will similarly tinge the whole body of common mercury. If he has the **star of Venus** <note>the alchemical name for copper</note>, in no dissimilar way, it will tinge the entire body of Venus into the finest gold, and into the highest and constant perfection: the same should be judged concerning the remaining stars of the metals, such as those of **Saturn** <note>lead</note>, **Jupiter** <note>tin</note>, **Mars** <note>iron</note>, etc. 

From these also, by the same reasoning, tinctures are drawn, which are by no means to be described in this place, because they are contained in the *Book of the Nature of Things*, and in the *Archidoxes* <note>referring to *Archidoxis*, one of Paracelsus's most influential works on chemical medicine</note>. By these few words, I wish the **first being** <term>primum ens</term> <note>the fundamental essence or "first entity" of a substance before it takes a specific form</note> of the metals and minerals of the earth to be declared sufficiently abundantly to true alchemists, along with the tincture of alchemists. Nor is there any reason why any of the workers should be deterred by the span of time of nine months <note>a reference to the symbolic "gestation" period required for certain alchemical processes</note>, but rather, let him proceed

<meta>This page follows the title and dedication of Gerard Dorn’s "The Monarchy of the Triad." Having established his patron and credentials, Dorn now provides a formal "Contestatio" or declaration, justifying his reasons for publishing these sacred secrets and attributing his inspiration to divine guidance rather than personal ambition.</meta>

66
## The Author’s Declaration

<note>The text begins with a large, decorative initial 'L'.</note>
I would have gladly, and indeed quite often, abstained from making public the sacred mysteries of God. But what else is permitted, or even proper to do, than to follow most obediently where the Fates lead? <note>original: "quò Fata trahunt." A classical allusion suggesting that the author feels a destiny or divine compulsion to write, despite personal hesitation.</note> I confess frankly that in this work, I act as nothing other than a writer and a scribe of warnings dictated by the divine spirit, as if I had been awakened from the deepest sleep. What can a wretched man bring forth from himself except mere ignorance? Or what does he have in which he might glory, rather than in having obtained grace from God and being deemed worthy of His service and obedience, to whom alone glory belongs? 

May He, therefore—who alone has the power and the will—grant that by such a happy guidance and with faithful companions, I may complete this journey I have begun toward that end to which He Himself wished to direct me by His command. It is not at all to be doubted that this work is for the Defense of Truth, <note>original: "Vindicias Veritatis." Dorn likely refers to the "True Philosophy" of Paracelsus, which he believed was being suppressed by the traditional medical and academic establishment of his time.</note> which has been oppressed by those who have falsely claimed it all for themselves until now. It is also for the liberation of those who, under such leaders, were so blinded that—though their physical eyes were wide open, their minds saw nothing—they may now see the light of Him who is Light itself, and who dwells in inaccessible light: <note>A reference to 1 Timothy 6:16, "Who only hath immortality, dwelling in the light which no man can approach unto."</note> to whom alone be honor, praise, and eternal glory.

<summary>In this preface, Gerard Dorn declares that he writes not of his own will, but under divine inspiration to reveal "sacred mysteries" and defend the truth from those who have suppressed it.</summary>
<keywords>divine inspiration, sacred mysteries, Defense of Truth, religious philosophy, Paracelsianism, divine light, 16th-century alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 68 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory front matter of Gerard Dorn’s "The Monarchy of the Triad." Following his "Author's Declaration" on page 66, in which he disclaims personal credit for his work, Dorn here begins his formal Prologue with a prayerful invocation to God, emphasizing the total dependence of human intellect on divine illumination.</meta>

67
# PROLOGUE

<note>The text begins with a large, decorative initial 'S'.</note>
Eternal God, the Highest Good: it is in no way possible that the human intellect, without the greatest consternation of its own mind, could consider even the smallest point of Your inexhaustible Wisdom. I say nothing of whether it is able to express it with a faltering tongue, or write it with a trembling pen, unless it be done by the command of Your Most Holy Spirit, impressing that very thing into the hearts of men—whom He also does not cease to warn silently and daily exhort toward the confession of Truth in all things. 

Since, if this pursuit ought to be attempted or carried out by anyone among the living on earth, it is necessary first of all that he be illuminated by the light of Your divine splendor, so that he may acknowledge the darkness of his own ignorance and frankly confess that he can be liberated from them by no means other than Your powerful hand. 

And if perchance, by the persuasion of the Calumniator <note>original: "Calumniatoris." A traditional title for the Devil (the Accuser), suggesting that pride or self-reliance is a diabolical trap.</note>, he seeks help from himself, he will find himself plunged into a deeper pit of captivity; for by himself he will never find any other means of liberation than the snare in which he entangled himself from the beginning, departing from the obedience which he owed to You, his Creator. 

The most evident examples of this are set before our eyes daily through those things which the writings of infidels, heathens, and Pagans have produced thus far from worldly Wisdom <note>original: "mundana Sapientia." In the Hermetic tradition, this refers to academic or "Aristotelian" knowledge that lacks spiritual insight.</note>: especially regarding the treatment of the faculties of the arts and—

Truly, such a riding of monsters is not without its omen. Why do we wonder if God sometimes rouses spirited horses—more impatient than asses—who are moved to kick against these monstrous offspring? For while these men rage so stubbornly, driven by pride and ignorance, they are met with resistance. If this race of men were worthy of the ass, they would certainly submit themselves to the yoke of patience, which needs neither bridle nor halter. They would imitate the ass in this very thing: that they would examine their steps, as the ass does, slowly and humbly, with patience, lest they slip into ignorance. 

That is to say, they would more diligently investigate their own foundations to see if they were built of solid stones taken from the Rock of Truth. OF THE TRIAD 71

"I am the way, the truth, and the life." <note>original: "sum via, veritas, & vita." A quotation from the Gospel of John 14:6. Dorn uses this biblical triad as the foundation for his entire philosophical and medical system.</note> From this we most firmly conclude that in all our actions, arts, and sciences, we must progress from the center of truth toward health and life, both in this world and in the age to come. In the first word [the Way], charity toward brothers is prefigured; in the second [the Truth], the foundation and end of our actions and works; and in the third [the Life], both forms of salvation <note>The Latin word "salus" means both physical health and spiritual salvation. Dorn argues that true medicine must address both the body and the soul.</note> are to be sought in the Center of unity.

We also have the most certain testimony, O best Father, that You created the Physician and Medicine, and that You will that he should be honored because of it <note>A reference to the Book of Sirach (Ecclesiasticus) 38:1: "Honor the physician... for the Lord created him." This was a standard justification for the medical profession in the Renaissance.</note>—a fact which no true Christian calls into doubt, provided that it is true medicine and not false. The difference between these two cannot be sought better or more exactly than from their own principles. Therefore, with God as our guide, we shall happily begin the anatomy <note>In this context, "anatomy" does not mean the dissection of a body, but rather a "dissection" or detailed analysis of the underlying principles and nature of a subject.</note> of these principles from both sides in the following sections, starting from the Unary.

72 THE MONARCHY
->Always like itself.<-

The image of truth, resting in the unity of its center, diffused into infinity, and visible to the mind on all sides only by a point (though an invisible one), is an exemplar most worthy of the highest admiration and praise.

### Chapter One.

**F**rom the beginning, before God the Most High would procreate man, His final creature, in His own image and likeness, He willed to create from nothing man's dwelling-place <note>Dorn refers here to the macrocosm, or the physical universe, created as a home for the microcosm, which is man.</note>, and within it whatever would be of use to him. Nor did He wish this dwelling to be destitute of His image, because He had established it as the first visible creature. This creation, formed in a most beautiful order and arranged by number, measure, and weight <note>original: "numeris, mensura pondereque." This is a famous reference to the biblical Book of Wisdom 11:20: "Thou hast ordered all things in measure and number and weight." For Paracelsian philosophers like Dorn, this was the divine justification for chemistry and physics.</note>, He decorated with truly admirable craftsmanship. 

He decreed to use these three instruments as if they were the primary ideas and forms in this work of His. From this we conclude, not unwisely nor rashly, that men ought to draw the principles of the foundations of their arts and sciences—especially of medicine (which first offered itself as necessary to man after the Fall <note>In Christian theology, the "Fall of Man" introduced sickness and death into the world, making the art of medicine a divine necessity for survival.</note>)—with the highest reverence and thanksgiving. This is especially true because we see that the supreme Creator used these numbers, measures, and weights, although He did not need them (for He who is God Almighty could have created all things by a single nod). Instead, He used them so that for us humans, who are capable of nothing by ourselves, He might provide exemplars...

<summary>Dorn explains that God created the universe using "number, weight, and measure" to serve as a template for human science. He argues that medicine is the most essential science because it was the first necessity granted to humanity following the Fall from Grace.</summary>

In this context, Dorn refers to the "number, measure, and weight" mentioned on the previous page as the physical "images" or blueprints of God’s mind.</note>, we will teach our medical principles, as is right, and as they have been drawn out, and this by the strongest arguments and irreproachable examples. Against these principles, the Heathen and Pagan physicians rage so fiercely; in opposition to them, we have decided to set down their principles likewise, so that both may be open to the whole world, and it may be free for judicious men to discern between the true and the false foundation.

We are greatly comforted that we depend on the fountain of Truth, and in this we have imitated Christian Teachers, and not Gentiles or infidels. Furthermore, our enemies can bring nothing against us except the authorities of men rejected by God, who were utterly ignorant of the truth that exists in God alone, whom they never knew. Let them show even one among their Teachers and wise men who knew God through Truth, or who was not a blasphemer against God and the Son of God—as was that <term>Prince of Medicine, Galen</term><note>Claudius Galenus (129–210 AD) was the Roman physician whose theories of the four humors dominated Western medicine for 1,500 years. 74        MONARCHY.

...led by a reasoning which they are not ashamed to put forward so impudently: namely, that in the arts and in the academic faculties, nothing is to be done concerning God. It is as if they were concluding that these subjects should be possessed and practiced without God. What blasphemy this is! From this, it is clearer than light that their physicians do nothing out of charity, but only out of greed. Can there be a physician and medicine without their Creator? But let us dismiss those men who even think such things, and let us come to the conception of the mind.

God, indeed, is a pure and simple essence existing through Himself, incomprehensible by any other, invisible, indefinable, and indivisible. He is prefigured to us through <term>Unity</term><note>In the Neoplatonic and Alchemical tradition, the number "One" (Unity) represents God—perfect, unchanging, and whole. All numbers after One represent a "falling away" from this divine perfection.</note>; which, being devoid of multiplicity, admits no contrariety, and consequently, no infirmity. Since the world was created by God in the image and likeness of unity, it persists in that Union, although arranged through numbers (as mention of these is made in <term>Genesis</term><note>Dorn refers here to the "Six Days" of creation, where the diversity of the world is established by divine numbering.</note>). 

Inasmuch as these [created things] would still retain the nature of Union under the obedience of man, and would produce no multiplicity, until man—seduced through the <term>Duality of the Binary</term><note>The "Binary" or the number two was viewed by alchemists as the origin of evil or division because it is the first number to break away from the "One." It represents the separation between man and God, or the conflict between opposites.</note>—was plunged into the contrariety and disobedience of multiplicity. Having been stripped of the garment of perfection, as well as health of mind and body, the wretch contracted the stain of every vice, infirmity, and disease. Previously, he did not stand in need of any medicine, until he fell into this disease, the first of all kinds of diseases...

<summary>Dorn argues that medicine cannot be separated from God and explains that disease originated when man abandoned Divine Unity for the "Binary" of multiplicity and disobedience.</summary>

OF THE TRIAD. 76

...[man] had fallen, deprived of all health. Since, therefore, the first origin of all diseases happens to man through his departure from <term>Unity</term><note>In Dorn’s philosophy, Unity is the state of being one with God. To "depart from Unity" is to enter the realm of the "Binary"—the world of opposites, conflict, and physical decay.</note>, it follows that all health consists in Unity alone, and health must necessarily be sought within it. Now let us see into what diseases man fell: namely, into shame, and the horror of death, and the fear of both. Therefore, being unhealthy in both spirit and body—because he would make his exit into both <term>deaths</term><note>The "two deaths" refer to the death of the soul (spiritual separation from God) and the death of the body (physical mortality).</note>, and soon after his fall would tend toward the destruction of both lives—a twofold medicine was needed so that he might be saved: namely, the Mercy of God, and Bread. Under the latter [Bread], the healing of the body is contained, expressed in these divine words of correction and punishment: <note>original: "In sudore vultus tui, vesceris pane tuo."</note> *In the sweat of your face, you shall eat your bread.* This bread is not only to be understood as that by which the body is sustained for nourishment, but also as whatever is necessary for the life of man. To a diseased and unhealthy body, the medicine for its life is bread; if the body is deprived of this, no differently than if it lacked food, it becomes subject to death. 

Truly, at the greatest cost, we have exchanged the bread of blessing for the bread of sweat, pain, anxiety, and care. Therefore, the "bread of medicine"—not that bread which is acquired through this <term>art</term><note>Here, "this art" refers to the common practice of medicine or alchemy as a trade. Dorn distinguishes between mundane medicine and the spiritual-physical restoration found in God.</note>, but that which is necessary for recovering health—is by no means to be sought in any other place than that in which it was lost to us: namely, in the <term>garden of happiness and unity</term><note>Dorn identifies the Garden of Eden not just as a historical place, but as a state of perfect "Unity" where medicine and life were originally whole.</note>, which we have undertaken to explain briefly.

<summary>Dorn argues that all disease stems from humanity's departure from divine Unity into the "Binary" world of duality. He identifies a "twofold medicine"—God's mercy for the spirit and "bread" for the body—as the only means to restore health lost during the Fall.</summary>

76          MONARCHIA

...we have undertaken. We previously touched upon how the world itself was founded according to the Image and likeness of divine **Unity**. Since, by its perfectly finished roundness, its pure and unmixed simplicity—devoid of the multiplicity with which it was endowed from the beginning of its creation—is made clearly known to us. For we see that a spherical body, however large it may be, when moved in any direction upon a flat surface, rests only on a single point; it takes this point for its foundation, and through it, the sphere makes its hidden center manifest everywhere upon its surface. Within this lie hidden great mysteries, both natural and supernatural, as well as the secrets of all arts and sciences. If the world, therefore, had remained in its <term>union</term><note>In Dorn's philosophy, "Union" or "Unity" refers to a state of perfect balance and connection with the divine, where no corruption or decay can exist.</note>, there would have been no need for a physician, nor for medicine. Therefore, the goal of all medicine is to lead the sick person back to **Union**, in which we have founded our medical principles. It remains for us to examine from where our adversaries have drawn their own ideas.

The likeness of the **Binary** departing from Unity, from which death took its beginning.

The image of the false, attempting to tear peace apart and to raise itself up as another Monarchy, was divided from the simplicity of **Unity** into the confusion of **Duality**; and it raised its head into four horns <note>original: "cornua quatuor." In alchemical symbolism, the four horns often represent the four elements in their state of discordant separation after the fall from Unity.</note>, upon which the ungodly have built their <term>Single Combat</term><note>The "Monomachia" (Single Combat) refers to the internal struggle or duel between opposing forces—such as spirit and matter, or health and disease—that occurs once the original Unity is broken.</note>.

### Chapter Two.

After God the Most High had created His angels and the ministers of His glory under the **Union** of His peace and concord, there arose from an accidental fall—hardly understood by the human intellect—the first among creatures: the confusion of the **Twoness** and **Duality**, an enemy to God. Because of the multitude it assumed through dissent, and with the help of opposition, it transmuted the created image of the **One** into the **Binary**, which is diametrically opposed to Divinity and fights against the Truth.

This **Binary** indeed became the devil and the slanderer <note>The Latin "calumniator" is a literal translation of the Greek "diabolos," meaning one who falsely accuses or slanders.</note>, even if only in his thoughts—namely, that he might build another Monarchy, either equal to or superior to God. But no sooner was this crime of <term>injured divine majesty</term><note>original: "læsæ diuinæ Maiestatis." This is a theological application of the legal term "lese-majesty," or high treason against a sovereign.</note> conceived than the Binary, cast down from heaven together with his accomplices, fell outside the light into the outer darkness. He paid the penalty of perpetual death without end of torment.

So that we may better declare this matter, let us bring forth the reasoning of the <term>compasses</term><note>Dorn frequently uses the geometry of the circle and the compass to explain the relationship between God (the center) and the created world (the circumference).</note>, whose one foot meets...

38          MONARCHY.

...comes to the center, as if to the Creator, while the other foot [of the compass] relates to the circumference, as if to the creature. Therefore, when the rejected angel <note>The "rejected angel" refers to Lucifer.</note> attempted to imitate his Creator in the production of the Universe, he fashioned a compass for himself within his own perverted mind. He was thus forced to build upon another, separate center, because two centers cannot coexist in one monarchy, nor can two circumferences. For this reason, it is rightly called the **Binary** <note>The "Binary" or "Duality" is a central concept in Dorn’s philosophy, representing the first departure from the "Unity" of God. It is associated with division, conflict, and the origin of matter.</note>, because it attempted to reduce the **Unity** and the true center to a multitude of numbers. Having assumed a false center, he drew a circumference toward the middle; when the foot of the compass reached that point, it was changed again into another center, and he delineated another middle circumference similar to the first. However, this circle enclosed nothing; it was interrupted and bore the shape of a falsehood. From this arose the image of the beast and the double serpent, raising four horns toward heaven, which crept forth against the true Monarchy. 

Having perpetrated this first crime—the root of all evils and vices—this kingdom, divided against itself, fell, along with its Prince Lucifer and his legions of fellow soldiers. We should not wonder, therefore, that in the Creation of the World (as recorded in Genesis), God did not say on the second day what He said on the other five days—namely, the words: *And He saw that it was good* <note>original: "Vidit quia bonum esset." Dorn is referencing a long-standing theological observation that in the Book of Genesis, the phrase "it was good" is missing from the account of the second day of creation, which Dorn interprets as a sign that the number two represents an imperfect separation from God.</note>. This was due to the origin of that number [the Binary], from which multitude, confusion, division, and controversy take their beginning, as was heard above.

# OF THE TRIAD. 79

It has been said what happened concerning the **Binary** <note>The number two, which Dorn views as the symbol of division, opposition, and the fall from divine Grace.</note>. We do not therefore conclude that "two" should be entirely rejected, provided they persist in **Union**, as "two in one flesh" <note>original: *duo in carne vna*. A reference to Genesis 2:24, suggesting that duality is only holy when it functions as a single unit.</note>. But whenever they are divided into a **Binion** <note>term: Binion: Dorn’s specific term for a corrupted or "doubled" unity that has split away from the source.</note>—that is, they create a double union—we steadfastly assert that they are rejected along with the **Binary**, together with their offspring, the **Quaternary** <note>The number four.</note>. This is the "fourfold" or the "variated," generated from that which was already doubled. 

Whenever, therefore, two or more observe peace in the truth of unity, we say that the **Duernion** <note>term: Duernion: A "good" duality where two elements work in perfect harmony, reflecting the original One.</note> is well-constituted, and it is not then called **Duality**. We must not judge the **Binion**, or the "doubled union," in this way, because there should not and cannot be two Unions, but only one—just as there is but one God, and there can be neither more nor fewer. 

We also do not wish the **Ternary** <note>The number three.</note> to be interpreted as "ternariated" <note>Broken or divided into three parts.</note>, because it is the offspring and the fruit of the **Unary** <note>The number one, or the Monad, representing the indivisible Godhead.</note>, which never varied from **Union** in the way the **Binary** did. 

To return to what we proposed to say: it was heard above that this divided **Binary** "Single Combat" <note>original: *Monomachia*. This refers to the internal struggle between soul and spirit described on the preceding page.</note> raised four horns, procreated by the birth of the **Binary**. These the wise heathens and Pagan physicians chose for their foundation, by which they might distract **Union** into four parts. In doing so, they attempted to surpass the Devil twofold; for the Devil only attempted to tear Unity into two (though he failed), but they have torn it into four. 

The example is clear: although God willed to create and maintain only a **single humor** <note>The vital fluid or life-force.</note>, these physicians established **four humors** <note>term: Four Humors: The traditional medical theory (Galenism) that health is governed by four fluids: blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile. Dorn, a Paracelsian, rejects this as a "Pagan" division of the body's natural unity.</note> to persuade those who are poorly-guarded and unhealthy.

<summary>Dorn argues that while "two" can be holy if united, the "Binary" represents a fallen state that leads to further fragmentation into "four." He specifically critiques the medical theory of the four humors as a pagan distraction from the divine unity of the human body.</summary>

80          MONARCHIA

to persuade. I wonder why there can be four humors more than there can be four drynesses, as many coldnesses, and as many heats; or four waters, four fires, four airs, four earths, and four worlds? What a marvelous confusion and absurdity, I ask? By what means do these wretches—Pagans and infidels ignorant of the Truth—strive to draw us Christians, followers of the Truth, toward their own insanity? They do not see that wherever they turn, they find nothing for themselves except restlessness of soul; they find nothing certain, nothing true, and nothing stable. Concerning such people, the Apostle <note>Dorn refers to St. Paul in 2 Timothy 3:7.</note> speaks in these words: "always studying, and never arriving at the knowledge of the Truth" <note>original: *semper studentes, & nunquam ad Veritatis notitiam peruenientes*</note>. This is because they have nothing unified in their studies, but all things are instead contrary. 

It is evident by their own example, when through the Maxim of their medicine they teach that **contraries are to be cured by contraries** <note>original: *contraria contrarijs esse curanda*. This was the fundamental principle of Galenic medicine, which Paracelsian thinkers like Dorn rejected in favor of "like cures like."</note>. What else is this but to confess that they are born of the **Binary** <note>The number two, representing division and the fall from God.</note>, which is the root of all opposition? I ask you: should opposition—that is, the infirmity or weakness of nature (which arises because of the opposition and internal war of parts departing from concord or union)—be removed by war or by peace? 

The wise and prudent man would say by peace, not by dissent. What is the difference between adding fire to fire, and adding disease to disease? It would not help them even if they were to respond that they extinguish fire with water through opposition. These wretches do not see, once again, that this happens outside of the **Monarchy** <note>term: Monarchia: In this context, the "Monarchy" refers to the state of divine unity or the "Monarchy of Nature," where all things exist in a single, harmonious truth without conflict.</note>. ### Chapter four.

Now at last it must be considered from where errors in medicine have proceeded, and from where all corruptions in the rest of the philosophical professions have come.           ### Chapter Six

To finally bring our principles into the arena <note>original: *palæstram*. A Greek term for a wrestling school or gymnasium, used here metaphorically as a place of intellectual combat.</note> against their enemies, we shall propose these theses for them to chew on. **Unity** <note>The number one, representing God and the state of perfection before the Fall.</note> by no means requires medicine, because it is health itself. **Duality** <note>original: *Dualitas* or the *Binarius*. In Paracelsian philosophy, the number two represents the first departure from Unity—division, conflict, and the origin of evil.</note> can in no way receive medicine, since it has been condemned forever without mercy. It is only the **Ternary** <note>The number three. Dorn refers to the Three Principles of alchemy—Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—which he believes constitute the human body and are capable of being healed.</note> that can receive medicine, because it has been deemed worthy of grace through Him who alone is merciful. 

Why then do the heathen doctors of medicine <note>Dorn is criticizing the followers of Galen and Aristotle, who based medicine on the four humors (blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile).</note> attribute health to the **Quaternary** <note>The number four.</note>, which by no reasoning can fit the nature of medicine? It stands condemned, along with its root—namely the **Binary**—from which it was generated; it is of the same condition and nature as its parents. For a good fruit is not at all produced from a bad tree. 

Therefore, let them continue to build their edifice upon such a foundation, and protect it with a roof exposed to the air, the winds, and the lightning of Jupiter <note>A poetic way of saying that conventional medical theories are unstable and subject to destruction by divine or natural forces.</note>, while we build upon solid rocks. We say and assert that there are only three primary substances in man—and these are not merely "qualities," but well-qualified substances <note>Dorn is distinguishing his chemical "substances" (Salt, Sulfur, Mercury) from the abstract "qualities" (hot, cold, moist, dry) of traditional Greek medicine.</note>—in which, just as health exists through Unity, so disease has its origin through Duality. The first of these [substances] is heat...

<summary>Dorn rejects the traditional medical theory of the four humors (the Quaternary), arguing instead that health is found in the alchemical Trinity (the Ternary) and that illness stems from the division of the Fall (the Binary).</summary>

[The first part] is **natural heat**, serving as the root of life; the second is **radical moisture** <note>original: *radicalis humor*. In medieval and Renaissance medicine, this was the "fuel" or innate fluid of the body that sustained life’s flame; its exhaustion resulted in old age and death.</note>, serving as the nourishment of life, without which life cannot persist; the third is **natural salt**, serving as a **balsam** <note>original: *balsamum*. Alchemists viewed salt as the principle of preservation that prevented the body's tissues from rotting.</note>, by which the preceding parts are kept safe from corruption, to which they are by nature liable. For no other reason do we constantly and fearlessly profess and affirm the three primary parts in which the **three principles of medicine** <note>These are the *Tria Prima* of Paracelsus: Sulfur (the soul/flammability), Mercury (the spirit/volatility), and Salt (the body/solidity).</note> consist: namely, natural **Sulfur**, **Mercury**, and **Salt**. In whatever body, therefore, these three come together through a state of union, that body can be judged truly healthy; but in that body where they do not, disease and the root of death have first entered in.

Therefore we do not deny (as our enemies falsely charge against us) the **four elements**; on the contrary, we approve of them much more soundly than they do themselves, since we teach that in each of the aforementioned primary parts, the four elements exist in a certain inseparable bond of nature. The separation of the elements is utterly resistant to nature, to which such a power has by no means been divinely granted, and even less so to men. Otherwise, nature would work toward its own destruction, which does not happen at all; rather, it labors toward its own propagation through their constant mixing. By this very fact, those "sowers of trifles" <note>Dorn refers to traditional chemists or pharmacists who claimed to physically isolate pure Fire, Air, Water, and Earth from substances.</note> are refuted, who prescribe the separations of the elements with the greatest apparatus, in any way other than through the removal of impurity from purity—while there remains nonetheless an inseparable association and natural harmony...

From this it follows that they were unable to judge individual things or nature itself except through the most deceitful shadows. It is clearer than light that for the joining of two things of different nature and property—or things that are in any way contrary—a **medium** participating in the nature of both is necessary between the extremes. ...let us grant that the hot, by its innate nature, tends toward dryness, while the moist tends toward coldness. The qualities of heat and moisture are contrary to these <note>In Aristotelian physics, which Dorn is engaging with, the four primary qualities are Hot, Cold, Moist, and Dry. An unlearned person will stumble over this stone <note>A play on words; the "stone" refers both to the intellectual difficulty and the physical nature of mineral salts.</note> once again, saying: "It seems contrary to nature for earth to be dissolved by cold, when it usually dries out, and for it to be liquefied by heat." This rash critic must admit that, until now, he has not truly understood the natural **transmutation** <note>The process of one substance changing into another, a core concept in alchemy.</note> of the elements. 

But so that he may have something with which to sharpen his wit, we will leave these and similar matters to be chewed on further—to be explained more broadly in another place—and we shall proceed with our intended purpose. 

**Salt**, therefore, when dissolved, takes on the nature of water; but when congealed, it possesses the property of earth. If it should happen that this earth passes into water, and this water into air, and that air is transmuted into fire, what shall prevent a return back to earth again? Nature knows best how to observe this proportion with equal weights. Therefore, we do not deny the **four elements**, nor their qualities; however, we do not confuse them as our adversaries do. Nor do we assume them to be the principles or the foundation of medicine...

...foundations, as they do. We only say that our **Sulfur** has a single proper and innate quality, namely **fiery heat**, inclined toward **earthy dryness**. We also do not wish to be foolish like those <note>Dorn is likely referring to the followers of Galen, the traditional medical authority of the time, whose theories dominated universities.</note> who claim that qualities themselves are diseases, or that they cause diseases, or that diseases "hold" qualities badly, or that they must be cured by [opposing] qualities—which, in the judgment of sound-minded men, seems poorly reasoned. <note>Dorn is rejecting the traditional medical doctrine of "Contraries" (e.g., treating a "hot" fever with "cold" medicine). The same is true for the others: namely, **mercury** and **salt**. 

This reasoning yields to no arguments—for instance, the idea that the nature of the human body is its own internal physician. This internal doctor requires nothing from an external physician except restoration, or as they say, **fortification**. This is achieved by applying a highly purified external medicine that is similar in nature—not just in its accidental traits—to the failing <note>original: *peccanti*, literally "sinning" or "erring," used here to mean a part of the body that is malfunctioning or failing to do its duty.</note> part of the body. From this, the body reassumes its weakened natural powers; once these are recovered, it is powerful through itself...

->**OF THE TRIAD.** 99<-

Since the foundation and root of hidden things consists in this, it is not without merit that the natural foundations of our arts and sciences must first be drawn out from here. What we previously did through numbers, under the concepts of **unity** and the **triad**, we must now do through the **center** or a **single point** <note>In alchemical geometry, the point represents the "Monad" or the source of all creation before it expands into space.</note>, enclosed within a **monarchy** <note>original: *monarchiam*. Here, it refers to a unified, sovereign system of natural laws.</note> under the form of a **sphere**. It is our intention to draw out the **triangle** <note>original: *trigonum*. The first "hidden body" or geometric shape that emerges from the point to create form.</note>—the first hidden body—so that from there we may prove the perfection of our well-founded medicine. 

We must judge that the one body of the **Universe** consists primarily of **three bodies**, for the reason that **one** can generate nothing from itself except **three**. <note>A fundamental concept in Hermetic philosophy: the Monad (one) becomes the Dyad (two) through reflection, which results in the Triad (three) as the first manifest reality.</note> 

Therefore, we say that these **three** consist as one body through a composition made by the **triple divine word**. It is helpful to explain the component parts of the universe using words other than those used by our teacher **Paracelsus** <note>Paracelsus typically used the "Three Primes": Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury. Dorn is attempting to bridge these alchemical terms with traditional elemental and spiritual language.</note>, in the following manner:

First, we say there is a **fiery substance**, containing within itself **life** and **motion**, or the **soul of the elements**. 

Second, an **airy substance**, containing the **nourishment of life** and the **spirit of the elements**. These two together have earned the name of **Heaven** and of the **ethereal region**. 

Third, indeed, is the **earthy and watery substance**, both together carrying the **body of the elements** under the name of **Earth**. 

It is clear from **Genesis** that mention was made only of **Heaven**, **Earth**, and **Water**; but of **Fire** and **Air**, truly no—

Now, because those two are enclosed under others, just as fire is under heaven, and air under the upper waters. <note>In the cosmology of the time, the four elements were often nested: Fire was thought to reside in the highest "sub-celestial" sphere, while Air occupied the space below the "waters" of the firmament.</note> Nevertheless, three bodies are first presented to us by **Moses**, namely **Heaven, Earth, and Water**. **Paracelsus**, according to the study of Physics, interpreted these with other names: **Sulphur, Salt, and Mercury**. <note>The "Three Primes" (Tria Prima) of Paracelsus. He believed these three substances were the fundamental components of all material reality, mirroring the Holy Trinity.</note> Therefore, let us assign to each of these three—when they depart from their due and natural proportion—a specific **triangle** <note>original: *trigonum*</note>, derived from the spherical body of truth. As can be seen in the symbolic figure, **a, b, d** represents **sulphur**; **b, g, m** represents **mercury**; and **d, m, h** represents **salt**.

When these are arranged into a triangle, they enclose another **triangle of health and Union**, **b, d, m**. From the center of this triangle to its corners, a circumference is drawn which passes equally through the centers of the other three **"sick" triangles**. <note>Dorn uses "sick" (*aegrorum*) to describe the triangles representing the elements in isolation or imbalance. True health only exists in the central "Union" where they perfectly intersect.</note> From these centers, straight lines drawn to the corners of the "healthy" triangle delineate an equilateral, six-sided figure of perfection. <note>A <term>hexagon|a six-sided shape often representing the macrocosm or the seal of Solomon in alchemical diagrams</term>.</note> Let this be a sign for us of our well-founded principles of true medicine within the triangle.

Let us consider other perfections in our equilateral triangle. We see that it is enclosed by four surfaces, each consisting of three angles. <note>This describes a tetrahedron, a three-dimensional solid made of four triangular faces. ->102          MONARCHIA<-

Now, let us examine the foundations of our adversaries, which are derived from the quaternary <note>original: *quaternario*; referring to the four elements—earth, air, fire, and water—or the four humors of traditional Galenic medicine</note>, by means of the triangle. We shall see whether the individual centers of their triangles are reached by a circumference drawn from the center of union and health. For this purpose, from an equilateral square, triangles *a, b, d*, then *b, g, m*, and a third, *d, m, K*, shall be delineated, which enclose the triangle *b m d*. Beneath the center of this middle triangle, which designates health, let a circumference be drawn. 

We see that this circle does not pass through the individual centers of the remaining triangles. From those centers, straight lines drawn to the corners of the "triangle of health" indeed form a hexagonal figure, yet it is neither perfect nor equilateral. From this, we must conclude on the contrary that the medical principles of our adversaries, being founded upon the quaternary, are crippled by the binary <note>original: *binarium*; in Hermetic philosophy, the "binary" or number two represents division, opposition, and the fall from the unity of the Monad into the conflict of the material world</note> and possess no perfection. 

But should they argue that we have chosen triangles that are not equilateral, then if they wish to be judged by similar measures, they must not seek their basis from an equilateral quaternary, but from one that is unequal and imperfect, as indicated by the letters *l, K, f, t*. In this way, the imperfection of their principles—which are derived from the imperfect—will be made clear. Whichever way they turn, unless they return to our equilateral triangle, which is born from a perfect spherical body, this quaternary offspring of the Binary <note>Dorn suggests that the four elements are merely a confused "offspring" of division (the Binary) and cannot reach the perfection of health without the mediating power of the Three Primes.</note> must necessarily collapse into confusion. But let them see for themselves whether they can escape ruin by any other path.

## A Recapitulation of what has been stated above.

To ensure that the <term>quaternary progeny</term> <note>The "fourfold offspring" refers to the four humors or elements of traditional Galenic medicine, which the author views as inferior.</note> of the **Binary**—that opposing side and enemy to the Truth—does not find an opportunity for slander, I bring forward the authorities of that pious and upright man, **Johannes Trithemius**. He was, above all others, most skilled in <term>attained philosophy</term> <note>original: "adepta philosophia"; a term referring to Alchemy and the secret Hermetic arts</note>, and through him, the things we have written shall be confirmed. He was neither a Heathen nor a Gentile, but a Christian, as is most clearly evident from his writings. These are the things he spoke, or brought forth only in personal writings to his close friends:

The **Ternary** <note>The number three; the Triad</note> must be reduced entirely to **Unity** if the mind wishes to attain a perfect understanding of these matters. For the **Unary** <note>The number one; the Monad</note> is not a number, but from it every number arises. The **Binary** <note>The number two; the Dyad</note>, by departing from **Unity**, is the first composite number. Therefore, let the **Binary** be rejected, and the **Ternary** will be convertible to the simplicity of **Unity**. 

Every number consists only of unities. Do not all things flow from **Unity**, by the goodness of the **One**? And whatever is joined to **Unity** cannot be different, but brings forth fruit in the simplicity and operation of the **One**. What is born from **Unity**—is it not the **Ternary**? The **Unary**, therefore, is simple; the **Binary** is composite. The **Ternary**, however, is reduced back to the simplicity of **Unity**. Again, **One** is a pure principle. The **Binary**, departing from **Unity**, is composed, because it is impossible... <note>The sentence breaks off at the end of the page.</note>

...tinctures all things. Simple Earth <note>original: *Terra simplex*. It is not numbered because it is simple in nature, yet composed by circumstance.
